#2957. Lakers' Luka Dončić 'feeling good' after injury scare, per report

Los Angeles Lakers star Luka Dončić had an injury scare against the Milwaukee Bucks on Wednesday, Nov. 11.

Dončić suffered a right ankle injury with 4:25 left in the game after landing on Kyle Kuzma while attempting a shot. The point guard was awarded a foul and attempted his free throws before leaving for the locker room.

Coach JJ Redick stated after the game that Dončić had an ankle "tweak."

"I'm good," said Dončić, who finished with 32 points. "Obviously painful a little bit, but I'll be fine."

ESPN's Shams Charania reported Thursday that Dončić "actually woke up with no pain.

"He's feeling good. He will go through his normal treatment process," Charania said.

Dončić is expected to play against the Phoenix Suns in the Lakers' next game on Nov. 15.

#2955. Christian McCaffrey says Kaelon Black makes RB room ‘really special’

Christian McCaffrey sees the potential for something exciting in the San Francisco 49ers' running back room, and rookie Kaelon Black is a big reason why.

McCaffrey praised Black's ability to provide a different level to the running game when he plays, highlighting how the two backs can complement each other by rotating throughout the game and keeping each other fresh.

"When KB comes in, it's such a spark," McCaffrey said, via The Athletic's Vic Tafur. "When I can come in and come out and get my wind, he goes in and balls out, comes out, gets his wind. If we can just continue to do that, man, I think we can be really special."

Black, a third-round pick out of Indiana, won the backup running back job during the offseason and has steadily carved out a role behind McCaffrey through the 49ers' first eight games.

The rookie delivered one of his best performances Sunday against the Las Vegas Raiders, rushing 11 times for 70 yards. McCaffrey added 68 rushing yards, a touchdown, two catches for 19 yards and another score in the 38-17 victory. The two combined for 138 rushing yards and 157 yards from scrimmage, giving San Francisco a productive one-two punch in the backfield.

McCaffrey has remained one of the 49ers' most important offensive weapons, but finding ways to manage his workload is important given the physical demands of his position. Black's emergence gives Kyle Shanahan another capable option who can keep the ground game productive when McCaffrey needs a breather.

If the two continue building on that chemistry, McCaffrey believes the 49ers could have something special in their backfield.

This article originally appeared on Niners Wire: Christian McCaffrey says Kaelon Black makes RB room 'really special'

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation and the Federal Reserve Board today published feedback letters for resolution plans submitted in October 2025. The agencies did not identify any shortcomings or deficiencies in these resolution plan submissions.

Resolution plans, also known as living wills, describe a banking organization's strategy for orderly resolution in the event of material financial distress or failure. The agencies conducted a joint review of the 2025 resolution plan submissions from 15 banking organizations with over $250 billion in assets.

In addition, the agencies determined that the shortcoming previously identified in the 2021 BNP Paribas resolution plan has been satisfactorily addressed.

#2953. Wolves 133, Mavericks 94: You Like That?

Do you remember the last time the Minnesota Timberwolves played basketball?

Since the Wolves last played, the Minnesota football team got flat-out embarrassed on national television, then went through all five stages of grief before building a time machine that sent them back to the days of Kirk Cousins, and now, in one fell swoop, has gone from pumpkins to the NFL's new belle of the ball.

And somewhere in the midst of all of this, Joan Beringer is officially no longer a teenager and is starting to go gray.

I'm not saying having no Wolves games is responsible for any of this, or that if we had played sooner, maybe Beringer wouldn't have had to have a birthday (is that how that works?). I'm not saying that! But man, does it feel good to finally have this team back on the court and on our screens again.

In their first game back in eons, the Wolves laced up and faced off against a team in the Dallas Mavericks that seems to be fast-tracking a soft rebuild. On paper, both teams are pretty evenly matched in several categories, namely FG% (DAL #4, MIN #5), turnovers (DAL #10, MIN #11), and steals (DAL #14, MIN #15), so the Wolves needed to win the possession battle.

Certainly, the Mavericks would be up for the challenge, so it would stand to reason that this would be a challenging game for the Wolves. Right?

To start the game, Jonathan Kuminga was on a torrid pace, muscling to the basket for a lay-up and cutting to the rim for a dunk off a beautiful find by LaMelo Ball, who proceeded to find Jaden McDaniels on a similar play a few possessions later.

The Mavericks settled in and muddied the game as the quarter went on. At the 5:16 mark, TJ Shannon subbed in, and PJ Washington proceeded to bully him to the rim for an and-one. This was a great quarter for Washington, as he finished with 10 points in the quarter.

Perhaps an accurate distillation of the rest of the game, though, is that those 10 points would be it for Washington, and the Mavs 24-19 lead would soon be relinquished and gone for good.

Ayo Dosunmu and the bench mob of Bones Hyland, Jaylen Clark, Trey Lyles, and Beringer burst the Wolves out of the mud like the Snoats brothers in "Raising Arizona", spurring an 11-0 run that got the crowd dancing on their feet. After a couple of free throws by Naji Marshall to stop the run, a Gobert offensive rebound off a missed Dosunmu corner three allowed the Wolves to close out the quarter with this Clark nuke.

The second quarter saw more of the same for the Wolves. After a sloppy turnover by Dosunmu led to a corner three by Zaccharie Risacher, the Wolves had given up 15 points off of six turnovers. Those numbers would balloon to 25 and 11 by the end of the first half, and it wouldn't matter.

Dosunmu continues to truck along as Mr. Consistency, a perfect cog in the absence of Anthony Edwards. He finished the half with 12 points by doing what he does best: being an absolute bully out on the break.

As for Ball, he was unconscious from deep, and finding cutters left and right. Much has been said about his fit with Gobert, and there were still a couple of miscues on the Ball-Gobert feed tonight. Near the end of the half, though, Ball slipped into the paint as the ball guided him around. He faked once, and then floated the ball up for a Gobert slam.

It was a thing of beauty, and exactly why sticking to the process matters.

Going into this game, Ball's assist-to-turnover ratio (1.62) wasn't pretty to look at, but for the most part, I don't mind it.

The way I see it is that they are mostly a statistical fallout of process-driven stuff that should temper with time. Some of the results might be an eyesore for box score perusers, but I love the process. I love that Ball is not afraid to continue to funnel the ball into Rudy, even if his individual numbers risk taking a hit. The box score won't tell the whole story. It seldom does. But Ball's commitment to the process should pay dividends as the season bears itself out.

The Wolves would go into halftime leading 67-52. And then…

Ball continued his unconscious streak from long-range, followed by a Dosunmu transition trey. Dusty May would call a timeout not even two minutes into the second half to try to stop the bleeding. That worked just as well as trying to stop a Beringer nosebleed.

The Wolves were playing loose and fast. McDaniels was gliding down the court for barely contested lay-ins. Kuminga was hitting threes, and the lead would soar to 32 points midway through the third after McDaniels was blocked, got the ball back, and then threw down a vicious slam.

Right before the McDaniels sequence, Beringer re-entered the game with a clear mask (!). Before the mask change, Beringer had just one point and zero blocks. After the mask change: 12 points and three blocks. Need I say more? That new mask is magic.

Although there were times through the third where it looked more like a horse race at Canterbury, with players flying around with their manes on fire, any and all sloppy play was a moot point, and the Mavericks would end up waving the white flag before the third quarter had even concluded.

The fourth quarter was mostly a non-event because of the score (Wolves 103 – Mavericks 69 at the end of the third), outside of a Gobert and-one off a Kuminga miss (where he converted on the free throw!) and a smooth jump hook by the magic-masked-man Beringer, but I wanted to shout out Isaiah Evans on his first official points in the NBA.

I know it was on a goal-tend, but still, a milestone is a milestone.

And a shout-out to Jon "Boog" Sciambi on the call tonight, his first Wolves game of the season. I know he is beloved in Wrigleyville as the play-by-play announcer for the Chicago Cubs. As a Twins fan, I hold no ill will towards the Cubs. It's that other team on the South Side that I have problems with.

In the end, the Wolves were able to completely stifle Cooper Flagg for 12 points on just 5-12 shooting. Seeing the Wolves, he was probably having nightmares about his last game against them, when he went 5-19 for 12 points. I guess this game was an improvement? Baby steps, teenager. Baby steps.

The Wolves were also able to silence Kyrie Irving, who up until now had looked rejuvenated and was outperforming his career shooting splits across the board.

Caleb Martin just about doubled his scoring average (seven points tonight). He should be proud of that.

But this was perhaps the Wolves' best all-around effort in this season's infancy. Off a long break against a good team, Target Center was rocking, and from start to finish, it was just a rollicking good time.

Right now, the Mavericks have 13 guys logging double-digit minutes. It's clear that Dusty May and his coaching staff have spent the early part of this season seeing what works and what doesn't. I'd expect the rotations to get tighter as the season wears on, as players fill out their roles and the overall numbers flesh out into something a bit more comprehensive. So far, though, May has struck a good balance of thinking big picture while still keeping his team competitive on a nightly basis.

In a fairly perforated coaching pipeline, May's seamless transition from college to the NBA has been nothing short of impressive. I was late to the magic of May, only first hearing about him last year as the coach of the eventual national champion Michigan Wolverines. Chalk it up to object permanence on my part, but I know who he is now, and it's almost as if he was built in a lab to know ball and coach ball.

Yesterday also marked the one-year anniversary of the Mavericks dumping disgraced GM and possible secret Los Angeles Lakers operative Nico Harrison. Going from Harrison to Masai Ujiri seems to have stabilized this organization. Finally, adults have returned to the table.

Enmities from the 2024 WCF aside, I'm happy for Mavericks fans.

The Timberwolves continue their eight-game homestand on Saturday, as Chicago O'Hare International Airport's Bulls fly into Target Center. O'Hare ranks first as the busiest airfield in the world. Meanwhile, their team ranks first in a bunch of categories you'd rather not rank first in. Liftoff is at 7:00 PM CT on DAZN-MN and NBA League Pass.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2952. Federal Reserve Board finalizes changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 9:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Wednesday finalized changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements. The two final rules are largely similar to the proposed rules from 2025. The Board also requested comment on a proposal to better capture differences in banks' business models to generate fee income.

The Board conducts stress tests to ensure that large banks are sufficiently capitalized and able to lend to households and businesses even in a severe recession. In December 2024, the Board announced it would modify the test to improve its resilience.

"The stress test is an essential component of our regulatory capital framework," said Vice Chair for Supervision Michelle W. Bowman. "Today's changes preserve its resilience by ensuring that it is transparent, granular, and risk-sensitive. The public will now have greater assurance that the risks banks take will be reflected appropriately in their stress test losses and their capital requirements."

The first final rule requires the Board to invite public input annually on the stress test scenarios and any material model changes. It also updates the framework that guides the design of the hypothetical scenarios and adopts the models that will be used for the 2027 stress test. In addition, the final rule makes several adjustments to the stress test calendar. The final rule also updates the global market shock component, which is a set of shocks to specific market components applied to banks with large trading books. These banks will now be tested against two global market shock components each year, and the Board will use the shock that produces the largest losses for each firm to calculate the firm's stress test results.

The second final rule requires the Board, when calculating stress capital buffer requirements, to average the results from the two most recent annual supervisory stress tests for firms subject to the stress test in both years. The Board will begin averaging stress capital buffer requirements in 2028 to ensure that only models incorporating public input are used in the calculation.

The proposal would request public comment on a revision to the Board's noninterest income model that would improve the stress test's ability to capture business model diversity across firms. If adopted, these adjustments would replace the current noninterest income model that projects each bank's fee income under stress. Comments are due 60 days after publication in the Federal Register.

Together, the changes are likely to reduce year-over-year volatility in capital requirements by approximately 50 percent and are not expected to materially affect aggregate capital requirements.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

Federal Register notice: Modifications to the Capital Plan Rule and Stress Capital Buffer Requirement

Federal Register notice: Enhanced Transparency and Public Accountability of the Supervisory Stress Test Models and Scenarios; Modifications to the Capital Planning and Stress Capital Buffer Requirement Rule, Enhanced Prudential Standards Rule, and Regulation LL

Federal Register notice: Request for Comment on Model Changes for the Boardâs 2027 Supervisory Stress Test

Stress Test Changes and Effective Dates (PDF)

#2949. Clippers' Bradley Beal still sidelined, getting 'not good results' in ramp-up

While the Los Angeles Clippers seem to be seeing strides made by some of their other injured players, Bradley Beal doesn't appear to be on the same trajectory.

Beal is exactly one year removed from having undergone season-ending surgery to repair his fractured hip. The three-time All-Star's hip has been cleared, but Beal remains on the injury report with right knee inflammation.

Beal has continued to serve as "a veteran leader" who spends time talking with the young guys, but when he will be able to get back on the court remains undecided.

"Every time we get him on the court to do something three days in a row, then it's like, it's not good results," Clippers coach Tyronn Lue told reporters on Nov. 12.

Beal averaged just 8.2 points, 1.7 assists, and 0.8 rebounds in his six games played last season before his injury.

For his career, Beal has averaged 21.4 points, 4.3 assists and 4.0 rebounds per game through 14 seasons.

Beal re-signed with the Clippers as a free agent in August to a two-year deal worth $13.2 million. The deal has a player option for the second year.

#2947. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2945. 2026 NFL QB Rankings: Midseason Edition

We are roughly halfway through the season, and it's time to update our QB rankings once again. I thought this season was going to be a fascinating season to rank quarterbacks because I felt that the back half of the top 10 was wildly in flux. I felt that the top 5 was pretty well established, and with Father Time catching up to Matthew Stafford, there's even a spot in the top 5 up for grabs.

But the biggest news is that we have knocked the King from the top spot. Patrick Mahomes has always been first on this list over the last two seasons, but it's time to acknowledge a fact: Josh Allen is now the best QB in the NFL.

Here's a list of the updated rankings, and as a reminder, these rankings are my opinion based on how the QB is playing this year, but also looking at their full body of work. Justin Herbert is not one of the top 30 QBs this season in isolation, but it's tough to rank him that low based on his previous seasons of success.

The King is dead. Mahomes is still great, but he doesn't impact the game as Allen does, and Allen has been at this level for the last couple of years. It's time to make the switch up top.

I think many of us keep wondering if we are ever going to get back to that peak Mahomes 2018 to 2022 version. He seems to be gone, but the 2023-2026 version is still very, very good.

Burrow is headed towards his first MVP, and it's one he absolutely deserves. Elite play without question.

In 2024, Lamar had 45 touchdowns and 4 INTs. That wasn't that long ago, but the injuries have slowed him down significantly the last two seasons. If Lamar has another injury-riddled season next year, he may start tumbling in 2027.

Brock Purdy has earned this spot with his play, but I also don't think he's truly a top 5 QB at the NFL level. Top 10? Yes. But I don't think Purdy is in this tier, and there are guys biting at his heels that could easily jump him before the 2026 rankings are finalized in January.

Caleb's play from week one and his three-game stretch returning from injury was better than anyone else's in the league. But 4 INTs, even if it's a blip, certainly has to be considered, and between that and the injury, it keeps Caleb out of the top 5 (for now).

I've been fairly critical of Love, but it's hard to argue with what he is doing with a suspect offensive line and no running game. The Packers have the best deep-ball passing attack in the league, and it's not particularly close.

After what Lawrence has done in the back half of last season and the first half of this season with Liam Coen, he has cemented Lawrence as a top-10 QB; now it's time to see how far he can climb.

Dak has led a maddeningly inconsistent and underperforming offense. He's still an excellent player, but he's most accurately considered a borderline top-10 QB, and that's where he's sitting midway through the season.

Stafford's season has been fine, but it has vibes quite similar to Tom Brady's final season in Tampa. The numbers are down across the board. They're still good, but not MVP-level like last year, and you have to wonder if this might be Father Time knocking at the door.

There may be nobody better operating from a clean pocket than Goff. Unfortunately, there are many QBs that operate far better under pressure. If Goff ever managed chaos well, he could climb as high as the top 5, but at this point in his career, that's probably not happening.

Patriots fans will probably see this ranking and be annoyed, but until Maye starts performing at a much better level against better teams, for me, it's going to be hard to push him into those elite spots on the rankings.

He's playing the best football since his rookie season, and he's leading a Texans team that we thought would be carried by their defense. If the Texans make the playoffs, it's going to be behind Stroud's right arm.

Daniels still shows flashes of who he was as a rookie, but a messy offense, injuries, and really poor decisions late in games are keeping Daniels down. He's going to need to string together a solid couple of months of football while staying healthy if he ever wants to get out of this middle group.

I still don't trust Nix, but maybe I should start. When the game is on the line, Nix makes the plays; he just doesn't do it for the first 50 to 55 minutes of the game. If Nix starts putting together a more complete game on a consistent basis, he can surge towards the top 10.

Herbert entered the season with a chance to finally break into the top 5 with a big season; instead, he's playing at a level that makes him the worst starting QB in the NFL. Tough to drop him all the way down with his history of regular season success, but he's playing at a level that nobody can consider him one of the league's top 10 QBs until he proves otherwise.

Darnold is another guy who had a chance to start changing the narrative around his name after two great seasons and a Super Bowl championship, but he has struggled this year and arguably looked worse than his backup, Drew Lock. If Seattle is going to get back into the playoff hunt, Darnold needs to return to form.

It's tough to have Hurts much higher than this point when the Eagles are clearly stocking up to potentially replace him. Hurts being out for the year with blood clots is awful for him, but based on where Hurts was prior to the issue, the writing does seem to be on the wall here in Philly.

Young had really started stringing together some good games where you had to potentially consider him being in the top half of the league's QBs, but in the last three games, he has 5 TDs, 4 INTs, has completed less than 60% of his passes, and a Y/A of 6.1. He's reminded us who he is as a QB.

Shough had a brutal game against the Vikings but has been pretty darn good in all his other games. He needs to start leading the Saints to some wins (which he could) down the stretch to start pushing his way out of the 20s.

Cousins has done a nice job winding the clock back and playing some pretty darn good football. He doesn't have a high ceiling in Minnesota, but he does have a much higher one than their other QBs, and he helps this Vikings team tremendously.

Rodgers isn't playing as well as some are hyping him up to be, but he's playing a lot better than most thought he could. He's quite limited at this point, but he can still deliver the football when he needs to.

Dart awaits his chance next year to show that he's ready to leap an NFL QB.

Baker is certainly showing signs of slipping. He's 31, has been dealing with injuries, and doesn't look like the guy Tampa has had the last three years. The Baker extension that he signed in September may age like warm milk for Tampa.

I think the question for this point with Penix is whether he is truly a low-end QB1, or a high-end QB2. He's the best QB on Atlanta's roster, but that QB room doesn't exactly have Joe Montana and Steve Young in it.

Geno is playing much better this year in New York than he did last year in Vegas, but he's still quite limited and not going to be anything more than a bridge QB for whatever team he's with.

Brissett lost his job in Arizona and is now going to try and keep the Ravens afloat while they hope Lamar can get back in December. Brissett has shown he's an excellent QB2 but really shouldn't be starting anywhere, and I'm curious if this is who Penix ends up becoming.

Watson continues to play better than what many thought he was capable of at this point in his career, but it's also safe to say that Cleveland is winning despite Watson, not because of him. Cleveland needs to find something better at QB; if they do, Monken could really take off in Cleveland.

Jones is the best QB2 in the NFL. He hasn't needed to play this year, but when he had to fill in for Purdy last year, he ran Shanahan's system fantastically. It'll be curious if Jones gets a chance somewhere to be a starter again in the next couple of years.

This is probably a little too high for Jameis, but when pressed into duty, he's been giving the Giants a lot more good than bad. It'll be curious what the Giants do next year. His contract is guaranteed, but they'll have JJ McCarthy and Jaxson Dart as well.

The Titans offense isn't exactly a juggernaut, but they have a pretty good offensive line, Tony Pollard, Carnell Tate, and a couple of other decent weapons. It's definitely getting to the point where the Titans have to wonder if Ward was the right call at QB. He just isn't taking that next step.

Murray was a better option for Minnesota than Carson Wentz or JJ McCarthy, but there's a reason they went out and got Kirk Cousins. The team wasn't going anywhere with Murray as their QB1.

Lock has really played well, all things considered, for Seattle this year. He's won all 3 games that he's had to play; meanwhile, Darnold is 2-4 when he's under center. Lock has established himself as one of the top 5 QB2s in the league.

It made plenty of sense for the Dolphins to give Willis a shot, but he's not the answer at QB. The Dolphins are going to have a top-5 pick (maybe first overall), and they'll need to look long and hard at the QBs available.

He has officially turned back into a pumpkin. The Giants gave Jones a big contract, and it blew up in their face. Those who do not learn from history are doomed to repeat it. And that's why Chris Ballard doesn't have a job.

Burrow has been as good as anyone in the league, but he does have a tendency to get injured, and having a capable backup like Flacco is a great spot for Cincinnati if he's called upon.

It's time to see what Mendoza can do and have an actual idea of where he should actually be ranked on this list.

Another very capable QB2 who unfortunately has joined Lamar Jackson on IR. It would have been interesting to see how Huntley would look in extended time.

Mariota has settled nicely into his role as QB2. With a guy like Daniels who can easily get banged up, like the Bengals, Mariota is a nice guy to fall back on.

Sanders definitely looked better than Watson did in his opportunity. It'll be curious to see if Monken makes the switch in the next couple of weeks to see if Sanders can spark the Browns, who have lost two straight and 3 of their last 5.

Wentz's performance on Monday Night Football was one of the worst by a QB we've ever seen. He's lucky he's still this high on the list.

McCarthy has certainly looked better in New York than he did in Minnesota, but we need to slow down on the praise. His overall numbers still aren't great, and he threw a crippling interception to end Thursday Night Football. He still isn't showing that he's a starting-caliber QB.

Daniels has played better this season than Mayfield; not sure if that's praise for a UDFA rookie QB or criticism for a veteran QB who just got handed a second extension from Tampa. Either way, he remains a really nice story this season.

Minshew probably should have been in the top 50 despite not really playing because he is exactly what you need out of your QB2. He had a nice moment in Arizona, but his season is over due to injury.

Remember when Davis Mills hit the game-winning touchdown for Lovie Smith and that gave the Bears the first pick in the 2023 draft, which led to the Bryce Young trade, which led to the Bears drafting Caleb Williams? Good times.

Keenum still rides on the list thanks to his performance against the Eagles this year. It's still one of the feel-good stories of the year.

I still think Taylor is a solid QB2 even if he hasn't seen the field for the Packers.

Bagent is still clinging to his top 50 spot on the list, but it won't take much to bump him off with guys like Justin Fields tumbling off the list completely; Bagent could easily be next.

I would suspect Mitch is actually a better QB than Cam Ward is at this moment, but the Titans need to see if Ward can become their guy, so Mitch continues to hold the clipboard and be a veteran presence in the QB room.

#2944. LeBron Log No. 4: A courtside sip, 16,000 makes and Cleveland's welcome

LeBron Log No. 4: A courtside sip, 16,000 makes and Cleveland's welcome originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James' latest week with the Sixers included a sip of a stranger's wine, yet another number no NBA player had reached and a return to the city where his career began.

It also included his worst performance with Philadelphia, followed less than 48 hours later by his highest-scoring game with the team.

Welcome to the fourth edition of the LeBron Log.

No chronicle of James' first season in Philadelphia could omit the time he helped himself to a fan's wine during a game.

James chased Tyrese Maxey's transition pass into the stands during the third quarter of the Sixers' Nov. 2 win over the Bulls. He collided with Philadelphia native Troy Clemons, spilled some of Clemons' wine and sampled what had landed on his arm.

The interaction received its own story on our site, and deservedly so. It also belongs in the Log.

Clemons did not keep the cup as a souvenir. He threw it away and got another drink.

James returned to the court and later broke into a dance after scoring over the 6-foot-1 Tre Jones. The wine clip generated millions of views across social media and became the defining image from an otherwise ordinary 19-point Sixers victory.

The following night in Detroit, James offered a broader explanation for why Philadelphia has already looked comfortable together.

"We've got a good team," James said. "Guys vibe with one another. Guys like each other. Guys enjoy being around each other. That has a lot to do with it, too. It makes the game a lot easier when you enjoy being around people."

Eleven games into his Sixers career, James appears to be doing exactly that.

James arrived in Detroit two baskets away from another unprecedented number.

His second field goal against the Pistons made him the first player in NBA history to reach 16,000 regular-season field goals.

James finished the Sixers' 112-106 victory with 13 points, 10 rebounds and eight assists. The near-triple-double helped Philadelphia improve to 8-1 and avenge its only loss of the season.

Kareem Abdul-Jabbar ranks second in league history with 15,837 made field goals. Karl Malone is a distant third at 13,528. James is the only active player with more than 12,000.

His scoring record has long been secure. James passed Abdul-Jabbar in career points in February 2023 and later overtook him in made baskets. The round number in Detroit still illustrated the distance he has created between himself and every other scorer.

James began his career by making 5,361 field goals across seven seasons with Cleveland. He added 3,510 in Miami, returned to make 3,691 more for the Cavaliers and made another 3,421 during eight years with the Lakers.

Basket No. 16,000 belonged to Philadelphia.

The Sixers' 8-1 start also carried a notable James connection. Each of his previous four teams to begin a season at least 7-1 reached the NBA Finals.

James was not ready to entertain that conversation in early November.

"It's too early to be talking about the Finals and things of that nature," he said. "We've got to continue to buy into what we want to do."

Two nights later, James returned to the place where nearly half of those 16,000 baskets were made.

His first game in Cleveland as a Sixer brought out jerseys from every stop of his career. Cavaliers colors filled Rocket Arena alongside Heat black, Lakers purple and the Sixers' throwback look.

James received a warm ovation when he was introduced last among Philadelphia's starters. When he checked out late in the fourth quarter, with the result decided, the crowd stood for him again.

James has experienced both ends of Cleveland's emotional range.

He returned as a member of the Heat on Dec. 2, 2010, to one of the most hostile receptions in NBA history. Six years later, he delivered the Cavaliers their first championship and ended Cleveland's 52-year major professional sports title drought.

"I've seen the depths of hell when it comes to a reception here, all the way to the heavens above," James said. "Anything in between is easy money."

The reception fell somewhere in the middle. His performance landed much closer to the bottom.

James finished with eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, three assists and six turnovers in the Sixers' 119-95 loss. It was his first single-digit performance in Cleveland since he scored three points against the Rockets on Dec. 30, 2004.

He had scored at least 10 points in 396 consecutive regular-season games in the city. The run covered his first stint with the Cavaliers, every visit as an opponent and his entire second tenure with the organization.

It ended during his first trip back in a Sixers uniform.

"I definitely did not feel in a good rhythm offensively," James said. "A couple of them were careless. A couple of them were just trying to make the right play and not being successful with it."

It was only the 10th single-digit regular-season game of James' 1,630-game career. He still took time afterward to consider what an eventual jersey retirement in Cleveland could represent.

"The journey from being an 18-year-old rookie, to leading a franchise to a championship," James said. "And everything in between."

James did not carry the rough night into Chicago.

Less than 48 hours after struggling in Cleveland, he scored a season-high 22 points in the Sixers' 127-121 victory over the Bulls. Thirteen came during the second quarter, when James helped Philadelphia turn an early deficit into a halftime lead.

His first basket produced another milestone.

James entered Sunday trailing Abdul-Jabbar's 1,895 points for the most scored against the Bulls in NBA history. His first-quarter three-pointer moved him into the lead. He ended the afternoon with 1,916.

The most interesting play from his night was a pass.

With the Sixers ahead by four and 2:20 remaining, James drove into the paint. Three Chicago defenders collapsed toward him. Maxey stood between the left wing and corner, but even he did not believe James could see him.

James fired the ball through the defense. Maxey sidestepped Isaac Okoro and made the three-pointer, pushing Philadelphia's lead to seven.

After his postgame interview, James pulled out his phone and watched the play again.

"He didn't think I saw him," James called out from his locker as reporters asked Maxey about the possession.

Maxey acknowledged that he had positioned himself to rebound because he expected James to shoot.

"Honestly, I was like this," Maxey said, raising his hands. "I thought he was about to shoot a layup."

James has spent the opening weeks of the season teaching his new teammates to expect the unexpected pass.

"You've got to become a wide receiver or a catcher at that point," Maxey said. "You never know when you're going to get the ball.

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced an enforcement action against American Express Company to address, among other things, the firm's failure to sufficiently detect and report certain suspicious activity related to money laundering.

The Board also identified significant deficiencies in how American Express Company's enterprise-wide anti-money laundering program was implemented, in particular at the firm's subsidiary national bank. #2941. Scouting New York Jets offensive tackle Blake Freeland

The Jets recently signed former Colts offensive lineman Blake Freeland to their active roster from the Bengals' practice squad. Today, we break him down in detail.

The 25-year old Freeland is listed at 6'7" and 302 pounds and was a fourth round pick out of BYU in 2023. He has started nine games in his career, all of which were for the Colts in his rookie year.

Freeland was a three-star high school recruit but headed to BYU with zero experience of playing on the offensive line having been a quarterback for two years before moving to tight end and defensive end as a senior. He also played basketball and represented his school in shot put and javelin throwing.

On his arrival at BYU, Freeland converted to offensive tackle but didn't see the field until the middle of his freshman season. However, he ended up starting the last seven games and held up reasonably well, as he only allowed one sack.

He didn't begin his sophomore year as a starter, but ended up making a total of eight starts in 2020, during which he showed promise by not allowing a sack all year.

In 2021 and 2022, Freeland started all 26 games and was twice named to Phil Steele's all-Independent Conference first team. He was also a third team AP all-American in 2022.

Heading into the 2023 pre-draft process, Freeland was being touted as a possible day two pick. However, his rawness was exposed during Senior Bowl week. Even so, he managed to stablize his stock with good workouts at the combine and his pro day.

The Colts eventually drafted Freeland in the fourth round and he started off his rookie year on the bench. He soon made his first start due to a week four injury, and by the end of the year had started nine games.

In 2024, he failed to earn a starting role and spent the first half of the season as a healthy scratch. He played in the last eight games, although he only played 23 offensive snaps, 22 of which were in the season finale.

In year three, a serious leg injury during preseason forced Freeland to spend the entire year on injured reserve and then he faced an uphill battle to make the team out of training camp this summer.

After being released in final cuts following preseason, Freeland signed to Cincinnati's practice squad before being poached by the Jets last month following Max Mitchell's concussion.

Let's move onto some more in-depth analysis of what Freeland brings to the table, based on in-depth research and film study.

Freeland is extremely tall, with excellent length, but was not strong enough when he entered the league so has no doubt been trying to add weight and is almost certainly heavier than his listed weight of 302. He managed 25 bench press reps at his pro day.

The rest of his workout numbers are impressive as he ran the 40-yard dash in under five seconds at the combine, then broke the combine record for offensive linemen with a 37-inch vertical jump. His broad jump of 120 inches was also impressive.

Having posted underwhelming agility numbers at the combine, Freeland did those drills again at his pro day and improved them dramatically.

Freeland has extensive experience of playing at both tackle positions. He started two seasons of each in college and has played both during his preseason appearances. At the NFL level, his first two starts were on the left but the other seven were on the right.

He would not be suitable for an interior role and has never played there at any level.

Freeland only allowed two sacks in his entire college career, with a low pressure rate. However, BYU's system operates with a lot of screens and RPO's so he didn't play as many true pass sets as you'd like to see from an incoming prospect.

Not surprisingly, he struggled during his nine starts as a rookie, giving up four sacks.

Talented NFL level players were always going to be able to exploit his lack of experience, and it didn't help his confidence that he faced Aaron Donald in his first NFL start.

Despite his athletic numbers, Freeland can sometimes be slow to get into his stance and susceptible to being beaten by speed rushers around the edge.

However, his biggest concern is his anchor. He can really struggle to repel bull rushers and speed-to-power moves.

Freeland's run blocking grades according to Pro Football Focus were very good when he was in college, but he was less consistent at the NFL level. The 2023 season when Freeland played extensively for the Colts was Jonathan Taylor's least productive of his career and he only had one 100-yard game which came on a day when Freeland didn't play.

Once again, play strength is an issue here, as he will get stood up on the edge and allow his man to fight off his block in the trenches.

He does give a good effort though, and his athletic ability means he can get out in space to good effect.

Freeland had some decent moments on short yardage plays, even though he's not much of a drive blocker. It may suit him that effort is arguably more important than technique in such situations.

Freeland obviously has the athleticism to get out in front of a screen and, as noted, it was something BYU did regularly when he was there.

Here's a play where he was at right tackle. On this one, rather than leaking out, he has an initial responsibility to prevent the upfield rush but then finds someone to block so the play doesn't get bottled up.

Considering his speed and agility numbers, it is disappointing to see that Freeland is sometimes stiff and mechanical in his movements.

One other obvious area that needs work is his hand placement. He can be slow to get his hands up and defensive players will take advantage of this.

His other big issue is pad level. This was always going to be an issue for someone as tall as him and he can tend to play with a narrow base so is often overpowered.

Freeland had seven penalties in seven games as a freshman but improved his on-field discipline over the course of his college career with just nine penalties in the last three seasons.

At the NFL level, he's been flagged six times. Four of these were false starts and two were for illegal man downfield. He has zero holding penalties.

Freeland's only role on special teams in college was as a blocker on the placekicking unit and that has been the case in the NFL too. He has had no issues or penalties within this role.

Freeland displayed good patience on stunts as a college player, and has shown he can handle those at the NFL level too.

However, it's in those one-on-one battles – especially as a pass rusher – where his lack of experience can be detrimental as he can be slow to react to or anticipate counters.

Freeland is a player who is known for his toughness and a relentless work ethic. He also has good leadership traits, having been a three-time high school captain – he played two years as a quarterback, remember – and also a captain at BYU.

To miss your entire third season having entered the NFL as someone who was considered a raw developmental prospect is rough luck and Freeland's fractured leg in 2025 has obviously hurt his career.

Prior to this, injuries weren't an issue as he played in 44 games in his college career despite being a healthy scratch for the first seven weeks.

As noted, BYU's scheme used a lot of screens and RPO's and, as such, probably wasn't ideal preparation for entering the NFL. However, Freeland has since had four camps with the Colts and spent a couple of months in Cincinnati this year.

Although current Jets offensive coordinator Frank Reich left the Colts in 2022 and Freeland was drafted by them the following season, there would presumably have been some overlap between his scheme and that of the incoming offensive coordinator Jim Bob Cooter, given that most of the key personnel was the same, so the Jets should have some concepts he's familiar with.

He was a teammate of current Jets cornerback Mory Bamba at BYU and his fellow practice squad members Jimmy Morrissey and Samuel Womack with the Colts.

The Jets picked up Freeland after Mitchell's concussion forced him to go on injured reserve. This meant that they only had three healthy tackles on their active roster, causing them to have to elevate Brant Banks from the practice squad a few times.

Once Freeland is up to speed, he'll likely be active on gameday and could eventually challenge Chukwuma Okorafor to be the swing tackle, given how unimpressive Okorafor has been seen leaving the Steelers. One of them might need to be cut when Mitchell can return, though.

Jets fans will, of course, hope that Armand Membou and Olu Fashanu remain healthy, in which case Freeland wouldn't get a chance to play. However, he could still be brought back as a project player next year.

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2936. Giants-Commanders: 5 plays that led to the loss

The New York Giants lost a close home game to the Washington Commanders, 24-21. New York's defense forced two interceptions in the fourth quarter — with a four-point lead— but the Giants' offense went three-and-out on both drives, resulting in an opportunistic Washington score that secured the win for the Commanders.

John Harbaugh used the apt word "disappointing" to describe the loss. New York was on the precipice of a commanding lead in the NFC East, but Washington now owns the tiebreaker between the two teams, even though New York leads the standings by two games. Losing home games in the division is tough, and something that's very familiar to Giants fans, unfortunately.

Still, the Giants do lead the division and have a tough stretch of opponents remaining on their schedule. They have two home games against the Jaguars and the 49ers in the next three weeks (a game against the Colts in between), as well as a trip out to Seattle. This season is FAR from over, but a win against Washington would have been a welcome, comfortable result. Here are five plays that led to the loss.

This was a three-play sequence in the fourth quarter where EACH side of the football failed miserably. Firstly, the defense shouldn't bear much blame for this loss; they forced two interceptions and a turnover on downs in the fourth quarter. It's the offense that couldn't pick up a yard and was stopped multiple times on third down when it mattered most, but I digress back to my point…

The third-and-3 quarterback sweep where Sonny Styles tackled J.J. McCarthy for a loss of two was the first of this terrible sequence:

Ar'Darius Washington intercepted Daniels with 2:19 left on the clock; 29 seconds later, the Giants are punting after three offensive plays for the second consecutive drive after an interception. I don't like the call at all, but the failed third-down conversion attempt was made much worse by a rare terrible moment from punter Jordan Stout.

Stout punted the football 12 YARDs from Washington's 41-yard line to their 29 — an inexcusable mistake. New York couldn't consider a 58-yard field goal attempt from Dominic Zvada, who missed a 33-yarder on New York's second drive, so special teams' unreliability compounded the problem, and this punt gave Washington solid field position.

The third play, though, WAS on the defense. Tae Banks committed a defensive pass interference against Treylon Burks, which gave Washington the ball at the New York 38-yard line with a minute and a half to go in the 21-17 Giants' game. Two plays later, the game was 24-21, Washington.

The Giants forced a third-and-4 with just over a minute left in the game, and New York rushed seven, initially, dropping two defensive linemen off a half-second after the snap. Jaydon Daniels threw it right past the Giants' defensive lineman to Stefon Diggs (3), who had inside leverage against Dru Phillips (22). Phillips fell and took out Tae Banks (2), allowing Diggs to walk into the end zone. The Giants capitulated the lead after several fourth-quarter plays that gave them opportunities to seal the victory. New York received the football back, though…

Washington ran Cover-0 on this third-and-10 with 33 seconds left in the game. J.J. McCarthy (18) had to heave the ball under pressure, taking a big hit in the process. New York was attempting to hit Malik Nabers (1) on the slot fade against Amik Robertson (2). Isaiah Likely (9) ran a quick curl and Malachi Fields (88) stayed on the line of scrimmage, which — in theory — would have given the Giants a true one-on-one matchup with Nabers against Robertson, but Rasual Douglas (29) had something else planned.

Douglas played off Fields with an inside turn, looking through the No.2 and No. 3 receivers (inner receivers, Nabers and Likely). Robertson read McCarthy and carried Nabers vertical, giving Robertson support on the outside. The ball was underthrown and thrown with a high trajectory, which allowed Douglas to squeeze Nabers' slot vertical; in doing so, he undercut the route and intercepted the pass.

Washington made a wise play by having Douglas play at depth and leverage to read Nabers' route and help his teammate on the vertical, despite being assigned to Fields. It's also something the Giants can learn from. Washington knew this from tape study, since New York runs the same slot-fade with the outside receiver staying put on the line of scrimmage.

The Giants had two solid offensive drives to start the game. On their first drive, New York punted from the Washington 42-yard line after taking a delay of game on a fourth-and-7 from the 37-yard line. They played the game of field position and did not attempt a 54-yard field goal. The Giants then got Zvada into a position to hit a 33-yard field goal, and the rookie kicker missed it wide left. It's just inexcusable.

TWO solid Giants' drives yield ZERO points because of the kicker, with one ending in the 33-yard miss above. John Harbaugh provided confidence to the media when discussing Zvada after the game, but these missed kicks by Giants' kickers are an all-too-familiar occurrence that is now persisting with the undrafted rookie kicker out of Michigan.

The Giants ended their first half by surrendering a nine-play, 67-yard drive that ended in this 1-yard touchdown catch by Ben Sinnott:

All three of Washington's touchdowns were on third down for Washington: the Ben Sinnott and Stefon Diggs' ones above, and the 31-yarder to Antonio Williams. The one to Sinnott, though, was in the final seconds of the first half. The Giants' defense surrendered the drive, and Washington bled the clock perfectly to not provide the Giants an opportunity to drive down the field for points. It was excellently handled by Washington.

An unfortunate bonus play makes the cut here. New York scored a touchdown on their first drive of the second half after the Giants' defense responded to the end of the first half with a three-and-out that lost 15 yards—wow! Unfortunately, Washington had an answer with a third-and-1 31-yard strike to Antonio Williams. that gave Washington a 17-14 lead.

New York, though, drove the football down the field on a five-play, 53-yard drive, with a big 16-yard catch to Malachi Fields on a second-and-seven. McCarthy then completed a pass to Nabers for three-yards on a second-and-12 at the Washington 19-yard line, and Nabers allowed the ball to get punched out of his grasp.

It was a red zone trip that ended in a turnover. Luckily the Giants' defense forced a three-and-out, but New York's offense punted the football — yet again — from Washington's territory.

#2935. Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup

Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers couldn't rekindle the magic of their comeback home-opening win over the Cavaliers.

They fell Friday night in NBA Cup action, suffering a 119-95 defeat in Cleveland. The Sixers dipped to 8-2 and the Cavs improved to 5-4.

The Sixers' top scorers were Jaylen Brown (20 points), VJ Edgecombe (19 points) and Tyrese Maxey (17).

The Cavs got 23 points from Donovan Mitchell and 22 from Evan Mobley.

The Sixers' injured players included center Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain). Cleveland was down big man Jarrett Allen (right knee contusion). Sam Merrill sat out the second half with a right elbow injury.

With Friday's loss, the Sixers are 1-1 in Eastern Conference Group B of the NBA Cup. Their next Cup game is scheduled for Nov. 25 against the Heat.

The Sixers will conclude their three-road trip Sunday afternoon against the Bulls. Here are observations on their blowout loss to Cleveland:

After a missed Merrill three-pointer, the Sixers steamed ahead on the fast break and Edgecombe opened the scoring with a slam.

The Cavs replied with a 15-0 run kickstarted by six straight Mitchell points. Much like in the Sixers' Oct. 22 win over Cleveland, they were many levels below their best in the first quarter.

The Sixers began 0 for 4 from three-point range, including two LeBron James misses. The Cavs were also the better team in transition and limited the Sixers' chances to attack in the open floor by taking excellent care of the ball in the early going. Edgecombe was called for his second foul with 7:34 left in the first quarter.

Meanwhile, Joel Embiid expressed his frustration about a couple of early no-calls. Embiid's larger problem was that he couldn't hit any sort of shot besides free throws.

He went 0 for 9 from the floor in the game. The seven-time All-Star finished with six points and seven rebounds.

Embiid has had a subpar shooting start to the year, especially from three-point territory — he's now 3 for 28 (10.7 percent) — but Friday was the kind of extreme night that's bound to happen here and there over the course of a long season.

Of course, it's notable that Embiid's been playing through right foot plantar fasciitis and the Sixers have been keeping a close eye on his minutes. We'll see how the team manages his availability moving forward and whether Embiid is able to bounce back next time out.

James didn't play anywhere near his finest game either. He had eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, eight rebounds, three assists and six turnovers.

As a team, the Sixers were 36.5 percent from the field and 25 percent beyond the arc.

Jabari Walker rejoined the Sixers' rotation as the backup center with Bona sidelined. Anfernee Simons, former Cav Dean Wade and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope all remained rotation fixtures.

With Walker and Caldwell-Pope on the court next to three starters, the Sixers cut their deficit to single digits in the second quarter. Walker threw down a fast-break dunk that cut Cleveland's lead to 48-41. He gave the Sixers some good defensive possessions when switched on to James Harden and provided his usual physical, high-effort work on the glass.

Edgecombe was fantastic in the second, sinking three triples, racking up 15 points and nabbing three steals.

Many second-year players would have a tough time finding a rhythm after encountering early foul trouble, but Edgecombe was great as soon as he checked back in and delivered a ton of valuable buckets in the Sixers' comeback push.

The Sixers pulled to within 66-63 when Maxey picked a steal and threw a pretty one-handed bounce pass to Edgecombe for a dunk.

With Embiid and James offering little as scorers, Brown hit several shots that helped the Sixers stay in the game. After his 30-point performance last time out against the Pistons, Brown reached the 20-point mark for a third consecutive game.

Still, the Sixers' offense never got rolling. The Cavs maintained their lead and extended it late in the third quarter. By early in the fourth, the Sixers trailed by over 20 points.

Embiid didn't play the fourth quarter and logged 25 minutes. He's had zero made field goals on just one other occasion in his career, back in Nov. 2019 against the Raptors.

Even with their tremendous first nine games, the Sixers knew they'd hit speed bumps throughout the year. They'll try to get back on track Sunday in Chicago.

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

Two longtime small-school standouts in Cuyahoga Heights and Mogadore.

And two veteran coaches in the Red Wolves' Al Martin and the Wildcats' Matt Adorni.

This should be one heck of a Region 25 semifinal.

We'll have live updates all night long from Cuyahoga Heights:

Watch Ohio High School Football Live on NFHS Network all year

The Wildcats last played the Red Wolves in 2022, when they prevailed 41-10 in a Region 21 semifinal at Twinsburg Tiger Stadium.

The Wildcats have gone on a number of deep postseason runs of late, including earning regional runners-up honors a year ago (as well as in 2021, 2022 and 2023). #2931. ‘Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics

'Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jaylen Brown was asked Sunday whether he and Jayson Tatum had spoken since the former teammates addressed their relationship during the preseason.

Does he plan to approach Tatum before Tuesday night's game at Xfinity Mobile Arena?

"I mean, he's on his side, I'm on my side," Brown said. "We're playing basketball. Both of us are trying to win. So it's nothing to talk about. Let's check the ball up."

After months of public comments, documentaries and questions about how their partnership ended, the basketball will finally take over.

Brown will face Boston for the first time since the Celtics traded him to Philadelphia for Paul George and four future draft assets in July. He spent his first 10 NBA seasons in Boston, played nine alongside Tatum and helped the Celtics win the 2024 championship, earning Finals MVP honors.

Tuesday's 8 p.m. game on NBC will also bring together the top two teams in the Eastern Conference. The Sixers are 9-2. Boston, riding a three-game winning streak, is 8-2 and sits a half-game behind Philadelphia.

"Another good opportunity for our team to be able to show what we're made of," Brown said. "I'm looking forward to it. Big-time matchup, two heavyweights in the East. Let's get it."

Brown has not hidden his appreciation for Boston, even as more has emerged about the deterioration of his relationship with Tatum and the organization.

"I love Boston," he said Sunday. "Boston's a great city. A lot of people I developed relationships with, you know what I mean? But I'm just focused on what's in front of me right now. And that's the most important thing."

He also acknowledged everything that accompanies an abrupt end to a decade with one organization.

"Decisions were made," Brown said. "It's life. It's business. Move on. It's OK. … It's emotions. You've got to pack up and move your stuff. Your family's got to pack up and move. But it's life. It's happened to numerous amounts of people, myself included.

"So I just look forward to being able to build with my team over here, trying to make the best of my situation."

Brown faced the Celtics during the preseason, but Tuesday will be the first meeting that counts. He said he did not know whether that would introduce emotions that were absent during the exhibition schedule.

"We'll see how I feel when we get out there," Brown said. "As of now, I've got no emotion toward nothing. So I'm just ready to go."

Tatum was similarly restrained after Boston's 117-97 victory over Milwaukee on Sunday.

"It's another game on the schedule," Tatum said. "It's the Celtics versus the Sixers. In the words of Brad Stevens, they all count as one."

Tatum called the Sixers "an incredible group" with a "great coaching staff," but declined to add to the discussion surrounding his relationship with Brown.

"Drama gets clicks and views," he said. "That's not my thing." CBS Sports reported Tatum's full comments Sunday night.

Celtics head coach Joe Mazzulla was even more concise.

"It'll be fun," Mazzulla said. "It'll be a good atmosphere."

Brown is not the only centerpiece of the July deal who will face his former team.

George has settled quickly into Boston's lineup, averaging 20.4 points and supplying the high-volume outside shooting the Celtics have long prioritized. He scored 27 points and made five three-pointers Sunday as Boston beat Milwaukee for its third consecutive victory.

The Celtics have not needed George to duplicate everything Brown provided. He has fit alongside Tatum, Derrick White and Boston's returning core as another willing shooter and versatile defender.

Tatum is averaging 27.1 points, six rebounds and 7.8 assists. His playmaking has been especially important for a team that replaced Brown's downhill pressure with more shooting and ball movement.

Boston enters Tuesday ranked third in offensive rating and fourth in defensive rating. It is one of two NBA teams currently inside the top five on both ends of the floor.

The Celtics have also made 15.6 three-pointers per game, the second-highest average in the league. They set an NBA Cup record by hitting 24 against Charlotte on Friday, then made 18 more Sunday in Milwaukee.

That presents a clear challenge for a Sixers defense that has generally performed well but has occasionally surrendered clean perimeter looks after committing extra help toward the ball. Tatum, George, White, Payton Pritchard and Sam Hauser can all punish late rotations.

The Sixers have reached 9-2 without depending upon the same formula every night.

Tyrese Maxey leads them at 26.9 points per game. Brown is averaging 22.3 points, 6.7 rebounds and four assists while shooting 47.7 percent from the field and 39.2 percent from three. Joel Embiid and LeBron James have supplied scoring, passing and defensive flexibility around them.

Brown has repeatedly described the team as unfinished despite its record.

"From our perspective, it's been some growing pains for sure, even though we've been able to win," he said. "The result, we're not so much fixated on the result. It's more so about the process, and we've got to improve our process and our mentality to creating a winning environment."

Boston should provide the clearest early measure of that process.

The Celtics play considerably slower than the Sixers, but their half-court execution has been more efficient. Philadelphia will want to create turnovers and run before Boston can set its defense. The Celtics will attempt to control the pace, generate three-pointers and make the Sixers execute against a defense that has yet to allow an opposing player to score 30 points this season.

There is also the unavoidable Brown matchup. Boston could use Tatum, George and White against him at different points, giving the Celtics several defenders capable of absorbing his drives without immediately sending help.

Nick Nurse expects Brown to bring something extra, even if Brown has publicly downplayed the emotional component.

"It's going to be very different for him," Nurse said. "I think probably a little bit of emotion in there, too, but also I think he's going to be very motivated to play."

Boston's rotation is healthy entering the matchup. Two-way guard Devin Carter is out because of an illness.

Dominick Barlow and Ariel Hukporti remain out for the Sixers. Adem Bona could return after missing Sunday's contest.

The betting market views the matchup as essentially a toss-up. Monday afternoon listings had the spread moving between Boston minus-1.5 and Philadelphia minus-1.5, depending on the sportsbook, with the total at 215.5.

That fits the larger picture. Philadelphia owns the East's best record. Boston has the conference's strongest point differential and has outscored opponents by 10.7 points per game.

The surrounding conversation will center on Brown and Tatum until the opening tip. Brown does not intend to contribute any ceremonial small talk.

"He's on his side," Brown said. "I'm on my side."

#2928. Michigan Wolverines arrive in Eugene before FAA grounding

It's shaping up to be another big Saturday in college football, including at Autzen Stadium when the No. 16 Oregon Ducks host the Michigan Wolverines.

As of now, that game is scheduled to proceed with kickoff at 12:30 p.m. PST, despite some of the travel issues other programs are facing across the country. The FAA issued a nationwide ground stop on Friday, causing trouble for some teams trying to reach their game-day locations, some of which, because of conference realignment, are multiple states apart.

Michigan won't be affected, however, as the team arrived in Eugene on Thursday before the nationwide grounding of flights, according to multiple reports. There has not been an announcement regarding a delay of the game on Saturday in Eugene.

The FAA is investigating a communication issue that is impacting all Air Route Traffic Control Centers, leaving the status of several games in limbo around the country. According to The Athletic, Utah, Texas, Tennessee, Ole Miss, Arizona State and Oregon State are among the teams that have seen their travel impacted ahead of Saturday's slate of games.

The broadcast of this game may be impacted by the ground stop. Tim Brando and the FS1 crew are in Los Angeles, slated to cover the UCLA vs. Illinois game on Friday night. It is currently unclear if they will be able to make it to Eugene for a 12:30 p.m. PT kick on Saturday.

There is no word on how long the ground stop will take. If the delay lasts long enough, it could impact Michigan's ability to return home after its game against the Ducks.

Oregon and Michigan are still scheduled to play at 12:30 p.m. PST at Autzen Stadium on Saturday. The game will be broadcast live on FS1.
