#2926. Celtics Mailbag: Legit contenders? Areas to improve, and more

Celtics Mailbag: Legit contenders? Areas to improve, and more originally appeared on NBC Sports Boston

It's been a frenetic and emotional — at least after all the hype in the build-up to Tuesday's visit to see old friend Jaylen Brown and the Philadelphia 76ers  — start to the 2026-27 season for the Boston Celtics.

Still, Boston arrived at its first extended break of the new campaign with an Eastern Conference-best 10-2 record. So how should we feel about that?

That was the biggest question when we asked a bunch of our Celtics group chats to send over their biggest queries through the first 12 games of the season.

So let's rip open the Celtics Mailbag for the first time this season. We'll get into what the two losses exposed, what Brad Stevens might actually need to shop for, and what the headband colors are telling us.

But we start with the question that gets a little harder to brush off with every win:

Have the Celtics established themselves as legitimate contenders through 12 games?

Look, it's probably too early to make wholesale declarations about what this team is capable of when the postseason arrives. Even after dealing away Brown, many pundits suspected the Celtics would be a very good regular-season team. But it's hard to argue with the early season numbers, including …

Boston is 10-2 overall, best record in the East

Ranks top 10 in defensive (second, 105.6) and offensive (sixth, 117.4) rating

A +12.2 point differential suggests a team on a 67-win pace

Boston ranks 26th in strength of schedule

The best teams each season tend to take care of business at home and feast on inferior competition. You can only play the schedule as it's drawn up. We'd feel better if Boston hadn't fumbled away a 22-point lead in Detroit on opening night, or shown a little bit better in Houston despite playing on the second night of a back-to-back.

Basically, we'd sum it up like this: If you said the Celtics were going to be 10-2 after 12 games to start the season, particularly with three sets of back-to-backs and some crazy early season travel, we'd probably have taken it. Boston has only scratched the surface of its full potential, but there's some very encouraging early trends, including Jayson Tatum's play, Paul George's availability, and positive contributions throughout the supporting cast.

Can we revisit the contender/pretender talk around Christmas?

What should be on Brad Stevens' early-season shopping list?

We've seen a lot of way-too-early chatter about Boston's trade needs, probably from people who put their Christmas tree up on November 1. We're not judging you, but we're kinda judging you.

One thing we do think is really important to keep in mind when we get closer to December 15 and the unofficial start of NBA trade season: The Celtics have heavy motivation to remain under the luxury tax for a second straight season, which would reset pesky repeater penalties and give Stevens an opportunity to potentially splurge a bit next summer.

The Celtics currently sit less than $2 million below the tax line, meaning something as simple as filling the vacant 15th roster spot would stress their budget. There's still pathways to bigger tinkering, but it would likely require sending out some of Boston's non-minimum salaries to add an impact player.

We do think that, given the glut of wing talent and only so many minutes to go around, even in instances where the Celtics prioritize rest for veteran players, there should be consideration of consolidating if a particular need exists elsewhere on the roster closer to February.

The roster, otherwise, is about as balanced as the Celtics have been in recent seasons. We wouldn't suggest there's an obvious need through 12 games. Some will lust for players that either 1) Put pressure on the rim or 2) Can simply take stress off the Tatum/George combo. Alas, those players routinely command salaries that Boston would struggle to absorb, even if willing to move on from core talent.

The bigger priority right now should be getting Mitchell Robinson comfy here, and figuring out the youngest players the team should lean into.

The Celtics have heavy motivation to remain under the luxury tax for a second straight season.

Did Detroit and Houston expose anything that Boston needs to get better at?

Good teams narrow your margin for error. So the Celtics might be able to survive their miscues and steal an overtime win against the Nets, but lose your way for six minutes against Detroit, then you've fumbled away a 22-point lead and the game.

We could playfully distill it down to Boston thriving whenever there's not a Thompson twin on the other side of the court. But what that probably suggests is that, when there are defenders that can challenge Tatum, it certainly puts a greater stress on the supporting cast to shoulder the scoring load.

In those instances, the Celtics need Payton Pritchard and Derrick White to be great, even against teams that might have the size and physicality to make both guards uncomfortable.

That said, Boston acquitted itself well in wins over New York and Philly, two teams with incredibly skilled starting fives. The schedule gets a bit more challenging through the end of November, though Boston might benefit from the fact that opponents will be dealing with some schedule difficulty or managing injuries.

A little end-of-the-month NBA Cup showdown with Atlanta should be fun, and there's a visit to Wemby and the Spurs right after. If the Celtics qualify for Cup quarterfinals (or beyond), we'll learn a bit more about how this team fares against top-level competition.

If you could lead the league in "being in the right spot on both ends of the floor," Baylor Scheierman would be among the league leaders.

If a playoff series started tomorrow, which young wing would you trust most?

The answer here is Baylor Scheierman. One of the stories we have in draft mode is basically how even with all these crazy analytics we pour over every day, we still can't properly quantify Scheierman's impact. If you could lead the league in "being in the right spot on both ends of the floor," we think he'd be among the league leaders. If you could quantify a higher-level hustle play — breaking up an alley-oop attempt with a deflection out of bounds, or starting a fast break by passing from the floor after recovering a loose ball — then Scheierman's stat line would pop a bit more. His basketball IQ is simply off the charts, and it makes him super impactful even when he's not shooting (though his 3-point percentage might beg that he hoist even a few more).

Hugo Gonzalez has had his moments. He was insanely active in that Philadelphia game. He's defending 22 shots per 100 possessions when he's on the court, which ranks in the 99th percentile for his position. Opponents are shooting 7.1 percent below expected on the shots he does defend, one of the best marks among all Boston players. You can make a strong case that Boston should continue to accelerate Gonzalez's development by carving out even more minutes for him.

Jordan Walsh has benefited from his starting minutes, and the confidence is growing on the offensive end. The Celtics have a real luxury with their wing depth but, when the games really matter in the postseason, we'd probably yearn to see Scheierman and Gonzalez dominating those minutes.

Who's getting to the rim when Jayson Tatum sits?

The Celtics rank dead last in the NBA with only 51.1 drives and 25 drive points generated per game. This is not wholly unexpected. You trade away Jaylen Brown, and you've got Tatum still learning to trust his body to attack the basket, and that number was always going to project low with a roster built heavy on 3-point shooting.

What it does demand is that, regardless of opponent, the Celtics need Payton Pritchard in attack mode. He's second on the team with 11.8 drives per game (Tatum is at 14.5). The encouraging number: Pritchard is shooting 46.7 percent on 3.8 field goal attempts off drives per game, and generating 1.2 assists off drives per game. The Celtics simply need him and Derrick White (8.8 drives per game) to put pressure on the rim regardless of Tatum's on-court status. White seems to have found his floater, which is a very encouraging sign.

The Celtics don't need to drive just to drive. But we know how important paint touches can be to creating opportunities for shooters. It's something to monitor, especially if the team downshifts and settles for perimeter looks when the offense sputters. That's when they need to embrace attack mode a bit more.

Can Paul George be the No. 2 on a contending team at age 36?

George is putting together an All-Defense-caliber season as one of the league leaders in deflections and steals. He's 11th in the East in scoring at 22 points per game. Would it be wild to suggest he's an early All-Star candidate based on Boston's hot start and the way his two-way impact has helped fuel it?

We'll see how his body holds up over 82 games. George surely was one of the best players on the floor when Philly rallied out of a 3-1 series hole against Boston last season, though the late-game suspension likely aided his body in being ready for that grind. It's slightly ambitious to ask him to do that over multiple rounds.

The positive outlook would be that the Celtics know full well how to bring their veterans along. Even if he's not on the full Al Horford plan in terms of rest, the Celtics will give George every opportunity to keep the body fresh over the regular-season grind.

Slice up the hierarchy however you like, but it's clear that everyone behind Tatum needs to be locked in and consistent for this team to thrive against high-level competition.

Paul George has been outstanding so far for the C's.

Biggest concern(s) through the early portion of the Celtics schedule?

We're admittedly nitpicking here given the strong start, but it's pretty undeniable that the Celtics have to figure out how to get the best version of Mitchell Robinson on the floor.

Whether Robinson is simply ramping himself up after New York's long championship season, or if it's simply navigating some bumps in the acclimation process, Robinson just hasn't been as impactful as even in smaller bursts through camp and preseason.

The fact that Neemias Queta and Luka Garza have both put up some of the best on-court numbers for the team so far this season has given the team some ability to figure things out with Robinson. Teammates gotta get better at lobs — though they've looked good when they work. We'd like to see Robinson crank up the motor and create post-up opportunities in transition like we've seen in small glimpses. We have to think he won't smoke as many close-range putbacks as he has through the early portion of the schedule.

It goes back to the overarching conversation here: The Celtics are built to be regular-season monsters. The question is, how do they transition that success and thrive in the postseason?

Some of that might simply be Tatum being great on the big stage. Some of it is making sure that players in the top six of the rotation — from Robinson to White to Pritchard — are playing their best basketball on that stage, too. There's a lot of time to work it all out.

Nerdiest stat in your spreadsheet to start the season?

The Celtics have worn white headbands in 10 of their 12 games. The green and black headbands are undefeated (1-0 apiece), but the white headband has brought out the best individual numbers from players like Scheierman. The NBA now mandates that players all wear the same color headband, so we suspect the colorful ones might be a bit rarer for players this season.

#2925. Which 49ers could be All-Pros in 2026?

As the midseason mark approaches, we're seeing some of the top award races in the NFL start to take shape, and the San Francisco 49ers definitely have a few worthy players in a couple of different spots.

Of course, there's Brock Purdy, who was the MVP frontrunner a few weeks ago and remains playing at a high level, while leading a 7-1 49ers team. San Francisco also has a number of other stars who continue to be among the NFL's best, which is why the team has regularly sent several players to the Pro Bowl every year.

But which 49ers are excelling at the top of their position to be a potential All-Pro in 2026? At the midseason mark, let's take a look at who could be in some of the award races and who could earn an All-Pro this year.

ESPN's Bill Barnwell compiled his midseason awards, which followed his quarter-season awards a few weeks back. Back then, Purdy was his No. 1 candidate for MVP. Now, he's still in the mix, but at No. 2.

"As a positive play machine, Purdy was my MVP pick after four games, having thrown one interception while taking zero sacks through one month," Barnwell wrote. "That's no longer the case, with Purdy throwing four picks and taking eight sacks over the ensuing four games, including San Francisco's first and only loss of the season at Atlanta. A bad Purdy interception with 2:42 to go and a chance to take the lead sealed it for the Falcons.

"Of course, Purdy is still running the league's lowest sack rate at 3.3%. And in terms of creating good plays, he leads the league in QBR and EPA per dropback. He's also No. 1 in success rate as a passer. And though Allen understandably gets plenty of attention as the league's most significant running quarterback, Purdy isn't far behind. He has been a valuable short-yardage option this season, with his 23 first downs as a runner ranking second behind Allen. He's third in rushing EPA behind Allen and Drake Maye.

"Purdy's numbers often get discounted by virtue of his offensive infrastructure, which is stocked with playmakers and operated by Kyle Shanahan. Though the 49ers have still had George Kittle and Christian McCaffrey on the field all season, and Mike Evans in the lineup for most of the season, Purdy deserves more of the credit for being the guy driving the offense. More than a third of his pass attempts have gone to players such as Jordan Watkins, Jacob Cowing, and Demarcus Robinson, and Purdy has made them look good in the process. Evans looked revitalized after a down season (amid injuries) in Tampa Bay in 2025. [Trevor] Lawrence and Purdy would both be legitimate MVP picks. I just lean ever so slightly toward the guy in Cincinnati being the best in football."

Joe Burrow's numbers have been ridiculous this year, and the Bengals are winning. So it makes sense that he's the MVP favorite midway through the season. But a couple of years after being on the MVP ballot, Purdy has a legitimate shot at being an All-Pro this year.

Christian McCaffrey will always be an All-Pro candidate, especially with the all-purpose spot, but it would be hard for him to earn the All-Pro honor at running back with the years Jahmyr Gibbs and Kenneth Walker are having. Those are the two Barnwell put on his midseason ballot.

However, Barnwell does have George Kittle as a first-team All-Pro at tight end, with Trey McBride coming in second. Kittle remains the best all-around tight end in football, as he does so much as a blocker. However, it feels like a part of this is because Brock Bowers was hurt for a few games earlier in the season, and with how statistics play into things, that might change by the end of the year.

Still, Kittle is firmly in that top three this year and could very well earn another All-Pro honor.

Trent Williams has looked like the best lineman in football this year, which is incredible at age 38. He was Barnwell's pick for Protector of the Year at the quarter-season mark and now at the midway point of the season as well. He's a clear contender for an All-Pro honor this year.

An underrated name at guard is Dominick Puni, who is quietly enjoying a very strong third season. He's the second-best player on one of the top offensive lines in the NFL, and has continued to ascend as one of the best right guards in the NFL. He wasn't on Barnwell's ballot, but with four guard spots on the All-Pro list, he could very well earn some votes this season.

On the defensive side, Fred Warner is a true contender for Defensive Player of the Year. Barnwell had him second behind Will Anderson, who is having a monster season for the Houston Texans. It's incredible considering he's coming off a major injury.

"I mentioned the five forced fumbles earlier, which is something we aren't supposed to see from off-ball linebackers. Those are the biggest plays Warner has made, but the future Hall of Famer has just been spectacularly efficient on a down-to-down basis," Barnwell wrote. "He has made tackles on 20% of running plays, the second-best rate among starting linebackers.

"And then, of course, there's no better linebacker in pass coverage in the league. Warner does a better job of taking away potential throws than any other linebacker, both by reading what quarterbacks are hoping to do and by running with potential receivers in coverage. His play somehow has not slipped after he dislocated his ankle a year ago. The 49ers are the league's second-best defense by QBR against play-action throws to the middle, where teams are trying to move linebackers and throw behind them for big gains. Warner is the reason those throws aren't working."

Warner is a clear All-Pro candidate and remains the best linebacker in football.

Those are all the main candidates for the 49ers, but they could realistically have six All-Pro players this season, with some fighting for bigger awards as well. Brock Purdy is in the MVP race. Trent Williams is in the Protector of the Year race. Kyle Shanahan could win Coach of the Year. Fred Warner or George Kittle could be Comeback Player of the Year after coming back from injury. And Warner could win Defensive Player of the Year.

There's been a lot of impressive performances for the 49ers this season.

#2921. Game Preview: San Antonio Spurs vs Golden State Warriors

Left for dead by plenty of pundits over the summer, the Golden State Warriors came storming out of the gates this season, led by the somehow still otherworldly play of Steph Curry and the surprising play of rookie small forward Yaxel Lendeborg. They were riding an 8-game win streak before losing both their match-up with the Houston Rockets on Wednesday night and Curry, who suffered an oblique abdominal tear just 6 minutes into that contest, never returning. Curry, who was averaging 30 points on ridiculous efficiency in the lead up to the Houston game, is out for at least the next 2 weeks, leaving the Warriors without the engine that drives their entire offense. Golden State to their credit hung with Houston most of the night thanks to Lendeborg's shotmaking, a focused team defensive effort, and offensive contributions from both vets and young guys alike.

Coming into this one on a 10 game winning streak, the San Antonio Spurs have been taking care of business. Facing Golden State without Curry makes them that much easier to guard, but head coach Steve Kerr's defensive game planning, Draymond Green's orchestration both on offense and defense, and Lendeborg's breakout is the sort of combination that could still make life rough on their opponents. San Antonio was able to sleepwalk their way into a big deficit before their huge comeback back win against the Chicago Bulls Wednesday night in Chicago, but against the well-coached Warriors that could be a recipe for disaster.

San Antonio Spurs (10-2) vs Golden State Warriors (8-4)November 13 2026 | 8:30 PM CTWatch: Amazon Prime | Listen: WOAI (1200 AM)Line: San Antonio -10.5

Spurs Injuries: Tobias Harris, calf (OUT), Devin Vassell, groin (day to day)

Warriors Injuries: Steph Curry, oblique (OUT), Jimmy Butler, ACL (OUT), Moses Moody, knee (OUT), Brandon Williams, hamstring (OUT), Gary Payton II, knee (day to day)

Golden State has been notoriously small for years, but they have just a single player on the roster this season that's taller than 6'8. That should mean open season for Victor Wembanyama, who has been on a historical tear to kick off his 4th season in the league and is coming off another huge performance in the win over Chicago. Draymond Green is still a versatile defender with an elite IQ on the defensive side of the ball, but at age 36 and in his 15th season, he is no longer the perennial DPOY-candidate he once was in his prime that could make life difficult on opposing bigs for significant stretches. Regardless though, expect him to draw a decent amount of the assignment on Wemby with help sprinkled in from Golden State's wings. Green and Wembanyama had a notable moment between them last season at the Frost Bank Center almost exactly a year to the day.

With Chef Curry out for the foreseeable future, the bulk of the offense could fall on the shoulders of rookie Yaxel Lendeborg. Lendeborg has been a revelation for Golden State, averaging 21.7 points, 7.4 rebounds, and 3.7 assists on 51% shooting from the field and 43% from 3 on solid volume. An older rookie at 24 years old, he's proven to be adept at attacking closeouts and getting into the teeth of the defense, using his big body (6'9" with a nearly 7'4" wingspan) to go at smaller defenders in the post, and seeking out contact and either finishing through it or finding his way to the charity stripe (6 attempts a game at the line). Opposing defenses have been preoccupied with Curry's offensive blitz to start the season and leaving Lendeborg as the #2 option, but that obviously changes with Steph out now. San Antonio has a variety of defenders they can and will send at him to see how he holds up as the focus of the defense. Should all else fail, they have the always lurking Wembanyama as the ultimate insurance policy.

Golden State has been among the league's most proficient 3 point shooting teams to begin this season, ranking 1st in both makes and percentage. That's largely (and obviously) been because of Curry's volume and consistency from distance, but it's not just about the numbers he puts up. It's also because of the sort of looks that his gravity is able to open up for others. Without him to warp help defenses and open up the easy catch and shoot opportunities for guys like Lendeborg and Brandin Podziemski, their offensive efficiency is likely about to take a hit. That doesn't mean that they still aren't going to launch them though, as they showed with their 17/51 showing in the loss to the Rockets.

If you'd like to, you may follow along with the game on our Twitter profile (@poundingtherock) or visit our Game Thread!

#2920. There’s a blueprint for beating the Vikings. Can the Packers execute it?

The Minnesota Vikings have built a truly special defense. They're extremely disruptive and not particularly interested in letting opposing quarterbacks get comfortable. Mastermind DC Brian Flores has taken the concept of an aggressive defense to a new level, blitzing on 75.3% of defensive plays this season. The idea is to create confusion, force errors, and keep down-and-distances unfavorable for offenses. And to date, it's working.

So why hasn't anyone done this before? Well, because with aggression comes opportunities. There will be some opportunities on Sunday. Can Green Bay's offense capitalize? I think so. Here's how:

To make Minnesota pay for sending extra defenders, Jordan Love needs to get the ball out of his hands quickly. While Love has a reputation this year for the long-developing big play, he actually has the shortest time to throw in the NFL this season (2.60 seconds). If you watched Buffalo's win over the Vikings on Monday Night Football, you'll have noticed that Josh Allen threw 16 passes within 10 yards of the line of scrimmage. Of those, 15 were completions, and the Bills gutted out a 23-6 win.

The Packers also need the Vikings to at least somewhat respect its running game. That's easier said than done, though the Packers did finally break 100 cumulative rushing yards against the Patriots last week. While Minnesota's defense is bordering on generational, they rank 18th in rushing yards allowed. They've surrendered at least 134 rushing yards in each of their last three games. Even modest gains on early downs can help avoid the long-yardage situations where Flores' is most aggressive.

Most importantly, the Packers need to win the turnover battle. Their Week 1 loss to Minnesota was actually extremely competitive for three quarters before a late strip-sack and interception helped the Vikings pull away (I will not complain about refs, I will not complain about refs, I will not com…). While not great, Green Bay's offensive line is much better now than it was then, particularly at pass blocking. And hopefully playing at Lambeau should make communication much, much easier. That dang horn, man.

I haven't even mentioned Cousins and the Vikings offense, because I do think the real key to this game is Jordan Love and company overcoming Brian Flores. The Packers don't need to reinvent the wheel. Their offense works. They just need to stay balanced and focused, and recognize the pressure so they can turn it against the Vikings.

The Vikings are thriving on chaosNo defensive coordinator has ever blitzed as much as Brian Flores, and he'll blitz Jordan Love with even greater frequency on Sunday. Here are the keys for the Packers.

Packers' offense vs. Vikings' defense: What's different from Week 1?"It's never easy going against this defense," QB Jordan Love says

Packers slot CB Javon Bullard plans to play through injury vs. VikingsCornerback Javon Bullard missed practice on Wednesday and Thursday due to knee and ankle injuries, but said he's planning to play on Sunday.

Matt LaFleur praises Jordan Love as MVP-worthyLove's success this year goes beyond raw talent. His head coach explains why.

Is Brian Flores actually a genius? Or is he just on a hot gambling streak? Let's talk about it!

#2919. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2918. TSSAA football playoffs scores from 2026 Tennessee high school second round

Welcome to the second round of the TSSAA football playoffs.

It's important to note two things about the outcomes across the state tonight.

#2917. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2916. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guidance; Extension of Comment Period (PDF)

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations (PDF)

Agencies seek comment on proposed third-party risk management guidance and issue statement on community bank engagement with core service providers

#2915. Steelers reporter doesn’t expect Derrick Harmon to return against Bengals

The Pittsburgh Steelers may have to wait another week to get defensive lineman Derrick Harmon back on the field.

Ray Fittipaldo of the Pittsburgh Post-Gazette said during an appearance on 93.7 The Fan that he does not anticipate Harmon making his return to action on Sunday against the Cincinnati Bengals.

"I don't think so," Fittipaldo said when asked if Harmon would play. "He's a young guy, and (the Steelers) usually like to have their young guys get at least one full practice in before they play."

Fittipaldo added that Friday's practice, where Harmon was eventually reported as a full participant, was a walkthrough and not the best indicator of the defensive lineman's availability.

Harmon has been limited in the Steelers' last two practices since his 21-day return window from injured reserve was open.

Harmon has been sidelined with a foot injury since Week 4, leaving Pittsburgh without one of its young defensive linemen. He was placed on injured reserve on Oct. 5 and has missed the team's last four contests.

With Cincinnati bringing one of the NFL's most explosive offenses into Sunday's matchup, Pittsburgh could certainly use the added depth and talent along its defensive line. However, based on Fittipaldo's expectation, the Steelers may have to make do without Harmon for at least another week.

#2913. Lendeborg, Podziemski set to give Warriors a glimpse at post-Steph Curry era

Lendeborg, Podziemski set to give Warriors a glimpse at post-Steph Curry era originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

For those curious about where the Warriors will stand once Stephen Curry hangs up his sneakers, the preview has arrived.

With Curry out for several weeks, the only two players already identified as members of the post-Steph era will be front and center. The extended auditions of Yaxel Lendeborg and Brandin Podziemski begin Friday night in San Antonio against the imposing Spurs.

Lendeborg, 24, and Podziemski, 23, have done well with Curry looking over their respective shoulders, but they're now on their own. If the Warriors are to avoid a disastrous slide, they will need sufficient contributions across the roster, beginning with veterans Draymond Green and De'Anthony Melton, assuming he replaces Curry in the starting lineup.

But the main characters are Lendeborg and Podziemski. They will have the ball in their hands as much or more than Green, but they produce much more scoring. Lendeborg is second on the team in scoring behind Curry, and Podziemski is third. Podziemski is second in assists behind Green, and Lendeborg is fourth. Lendeborg is first in rebounding, and Podziemski is third.

The most telling statistical category is minutes per game, because it implies an inherent trust from the coaching staff and value within the franchise. Podziemski leads all Warriors, averaging 33.4 per game, and Lendeborg is second at 32.3.

Podziemski's $110 million contract extension suggests the franchise considers him a keeper. This lavish commitment is no guarantee – see Poole, Jordan – that Podziemski will be a Warrior in 2028, much less 2031. Yet his is only the second large long-term rookie extension from Golden State since Klay Thompson received a four-year commitment in 2014.

So, yes, the Warriors believe Podziemski is as a keeper. At least for now.

"We think Brandin is a hell of a young player, a big part of our future, and he secures his future financially," coach Steve Kerr said last month after the extension was official. "And it's a deal that makes a lot of sense based on our numbers."

Those numbers relate mostly to Podziemski's positive impact on the court. He led the team with a plus-264 plus/minus as a rookie in 2023-24, ranked third on the team in his second season, dipped to eighth last season but this season ranks behind only Curry.

When Curry was sidelined for two months last season, Podziemski's scoring ticked up 2.2 points per game, but his efficiency tumbled, as he posted splits of 44.8/34.8 percent. He's at 48.1/39.7 percent this season.

The question before Podziemski now is whether he is better equipped to maintain similar scoring efficiency without the space afforded by Curry's famous gravity. And can the fourth-year guard improve his 69-percent free-throw shooting?

The question before Lendeborg is whether he can handle the burden that comes with rising from No. 2 on the scouting report to No. 1. It's a lot for anyone unaccustomed to it, perhaps even more for a rookie. The spotlight now is much brighter than any he has seen. He felt it after Curry left the game Wednesday night in Houston and responded with a game-high 25 points and team-high 10 rebounds.

"He really took a lot of the offense on his shoulders, and he's obviously very capable," Kerr told reporters at Toyota Center.

Lendeborg in his first 12 NBA games has passed, if not aced, every offensive test thrown his way so far. It's a thin resumé, but it already is delighting the Warriors and their fans, who are falling in love with his potential.

Kerr was quick to place the ultimate faith in Lendeborg's ability, elevating him to primary offensive engine when Curry is off the floor. Lendeborg's response has been 21.7 points per game on 51.8/42.6 shooting splits, while adding 7.4 rebounds and 3.7 assists. His Rookie of the Year candidacy is strong.

A lot must happen for the Warriors to win at least half their games without Curry. Green must take and make more shots. Melton, shooting 42.9 percent from deep, must stay close to that level. Georges Niang and Al Horford must produce off the bench. Charles Bassey must remain a force in the paint. Brandon Williams must return soon.

"We have to keep working on our offensive execution," Kerr said in Houston. "… Without Steph, we have to be organized in our concepts and with our spacing so that we can attack and create shots for each other."

Much of that task will fall on Lendeborg and Podziemski. If they handle it well, the Warriors can be respectable without their superstar.

#2907. Bill Simmons already has the perfect Pelicans’ Dejounte Murray trade ready to go

Bill Simmons already has the perfect Pelicans' Dejounte Murray trade ready to go appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The New Orleans Pelicans have been on a rough stretch this week, and it ended with Dejounte Murray requesting a trade from the team. Throughout the week, Murray has posted now-deleted tweets regarding the team, while mentioning some sort of "sabotage" going on within the organization.

The Phoenix Suns have been rumored as one of the teams interested in Murray, and it makes sense why Bill Simmons came up with this trade idea on the spot.

"Let's just call in the Jalen Green/Dejounte Murray trade to Silver already, why are we waiting for the weekend?" Simmons wrote on X, formerly Twitter.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

Ironically enough, Green was just mentioned in trade rumors as the Suns are looking to improve their roster as they're currently 5-6.

"Who could potentially be moved in Phoenix if this team is going to be sellers?" Brett Siegel of Clutch Points said. "Well, the Suns got Miles Bridges, and they didn't give him a new contract yet… Another one is Jalen Green, he is making $36.2 million this year, and next year he has a $36 million player option."

It would make sense for the Suns and Pelicans to do a swap of Green and Murray, but it's uncertain if the Pelicans have interest in the guard. If they do, that could make everybody's job a little bit easier.

The Pelicans are looking to get back on track as they're currently on a four-game losing streak. After their latest loss against the Brooklyn Nets, Zion Williamson called out the team.

"As men we have to, as a unit, players, coaches, everybody, you have to go look yourself in the mirror when you by yourself and say, did I do everything that I needed to do for my team?" Williamson said. "Did I do — was I in the gaps on defense? Was I a low man when I was supposed to? Did I get out there and contest shots to make it tough on them? Did I do everything I needed to do? Did I give in real effort?"

#2906. By the Numbers: Is the Packers’ offense sustainable?

As I tend to do every week, I found myself talking about the Green Bay Packers a lot this week. Some of that was just muttering to myself in the hallway of my house, but sometimes I was talking to real, live people and sometimes those conversations were recorded. One question that keeps coming up has been this: is the Packers offense sustainable?

On its face, the answer is "probably not." We're coming off a performance where Jordan Love had fire shooting from his hands: completing 79.3% of his passes for 372 yards and 3 TDs. He posted that 79.2% completion rate while bombing: 27.6% of his attempts were 20+ yards down the field (his highest rate of the season).

The question of sustainability revolves around Jordan Love ("can he keep this up"), but it's really more about the run game. Can an offense continue to be successful into the playoffs with a high pass rate and a bad running game?

No matter where you look, the run game metrics are bad. They're dead-last in EPA per Play at -0.26 (per NFL Pro). They're dead last in DVOA at -32.6% (per FTN). They're 31st in Yards Per Attempt at 3.0 (per Pro Football Reference). Pick your favorite football analytics site and they're going to be right near the bottom in rushing on every single one of them.

In looking at the overall team, I believe it's sustainable. On top of being an aggressive attacker down the field, Love has become a very good operator in the quick game, and the Packers have the offensive pieces to make that work. Whether that's working an old favorite like Slant/Flat or hitting some of those quick outs he has become so fond of, it's an area of his game that has seen a ton of improvement over the years. If they need to supplement the lack of a run game with a quick pass game – and take enough deep shots to keep defenses from sitting on the underneath routes – that feels like it could work. Combine that with a defense that has been playing extremely well (2nd most turnovers, 5th in DVOA, 9th in EPA per Play) and that feels like a recipe for sustainability. But is it really?

I dug into some numbers to see if that's something that we've seen before. Have we seen a team this bad at running the ball find success? I had to define success, so I determined that any team that made the playoffs could be assumed to have some sort of sustained success. So I grabbed every team that made the playoffs from 1999-2025 and looked at a handful of their metrics during the regular season to see if this is something that has happened. If you're asking "why 1999", the answer is because that's the furthest back the data I am using for this goes back.

The main metrics I looked at were Pass Rate, EPA and Success Rate.

Here's where the Packers sit. For these numbers, I'm only looking at games after Week 3, since the offensive line was non-functional for the first few weeks. Is that cheating? Maybe! But it felt like the best thing to do, and I'm making the rules here.

We're going to lock that Packers line so we can keep it in mind, then look at some other teams that may fit the bill. If there is a * next to a team name, it means that team was in the Super Bowl that season.

These are the highest pass rate teams in our group. As you can see, a pass rate of 61.1% isn't crazy given some of these other teams. It's still relatively high – a pass rate of 61.1% would rank 97th highest out of 336 teams – but it's not some crazy outlier. Among these teams, the Packers' EPA per Pass far exceeds the other teams, but their running game is worse than all of them except for the 2022 Bucs. Their Passing Success is better than everyone except the 2021 Bucs and the 2011 Lions, but their running game is worse than all of them in terms of success.

That led me to look at Rush Success Rate. Where does the rushing success rate from this year's Packers team stack up against our sample?

The Packers would still be a bit of an outlier – their 36.3% rush success would be the 22nd worst success rate among our 336 teams – but it has been done. The one that jumps out (because of course it does) is the 2007 Packers. I'm not alone in believing that, if they hadn't lost to the Giants, they would have won the Super Bowl that year. For this year's Packers to be significantly better in EPA per Pass – and slightly better in Rush Success Rate – is pretty surprising. That team also had a slightly higher Pass Rate than this year's Packers, as well. That 2007 team had a better Pass Success Rate, so they were better at moving the chains, but this team is better at generating points. If you want to point to a volatility point, something like that would be it. A high EPA per Play but low Success Rate could suggest that a team is punching a bit above their weight.

We could do this all day, but we'll end with this. I wanted to see what teams had a Rushing EPA worse than this year's Packers:

Those are all the teams with a worse Rush EPA than this year's Packers. A few of those teams (2022 Bucs, 1999 Lions, 2014 Colts) had a higher pass rate, but most of them were more run-heavy than this year's Packers, despite having worse results while doing so.

Of these teams, the Packers outpaced all of them in EPA per Pass and Pass Success Rate. They have a better overall Success Rate than all but 2 of these teams (2022 Bucs, 2014 Colts).

We'll stop here, or we'll be here all day. There's more that can be combed through, but I think we have a pretty good idea of where this Packers team stands. Let's close by revisiting our question:

Question: Is this Packers offense sustainable?

Answer: History tells us that it is, but it's also a fairly narrow path to walk. Teams with this makeup have been "sustainable" enough to make the playoffs numerous times, but it's not the norm.

Albums listened to: Blondshell – Violins; Leem of Earth – See; Remi Wolf – Mud

#2902. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2896. PFF grades and advanced stats from the Giants’ loss to the Commanders

The post-mortem on a game the New York Giants should have won but didn't is never fun. Let's see what we can learn from the Pro Football Focus grades and advanced stats about who played well and who didn't. Before looking at the offense and defense, let's note that PFF gave Jordan Stout his lowest grade of the season because of that 12-yard shank in the fourth quarter that gave the Commanders decent field position to start their winning drive, and of course Dominic Zvada got a low grade for his field goal miss from 32 yards out (though not as low as the first Washington and the Houston games in which he had multiple misses). That's part of the story, but there was blame to go around on the offense and defense. And let's not forget the coaching. In the old days we used to speak of a "prevent defense" that tried to hold a lead by playing soft coverage that stopped big plays. Well, last night John Harbaugh played what might be called a "prevent offense," being scared off field goal attempts and punting in Commanders' territory after Zvada'a early miss and trying to wind down the clock with running plays and quarterback keepers instead of calling easy pitch-and-catch plays to keep the ball out of Jayden Daniels' hands.

PFF did not give a single elite (80 or higher) grade to anyone on the offense last night (part of that was due to the weather conditions, Washington only had one elite-graded offensive player themselves, Ben Sinnott). J.J. McCarthy received an overall very good offense grade, more because of his running (three scrambles for 29 yards) than his passing. However, his passing grade was brought down by his final offensive play, an interception deep downfield on which he was blitzed and on which Malik Nabers did not make much of an effort to help him out. Overall, J.J. handled the pressure well, doing about as well as when he was kept clean:

J.J. was blitzed on 45% of dropbacks: 2 of his 3 turnover-worthy plays came on blitzes, but 2 of his 3 big-time throws also came on blitzes. Overall, with 293 yards, a pretty good performance.

Of note is the performance of the offensive line. Even though McCarthy was pressured on 35% of his dropbacks, the OL performed pretty well overall. Jon Runyan and Sisi Mauigoa had great pass-blocking games, Runyan having a clean stat line and Mauigoa only giving up one hurry. The run block grades weren't as good but not terrible either. Andrew Thomas and Jermaine Eluemunor both graded very good in pass coverage with 2 hurries charged to AT and one QB hit to Eluemunor. Bryan Hudson graded below average in pass blocking with 2 hurries and just barely below average in run blocking. Patrick Ricard had his best game as a Giant, especially in run blocking. Cam Skattebo fell just below the Very Good category with his 18 rushes for 78 yards; maybe he was graded down a bit for eating before the game and throwing up on the sideline 🙂

None of the receivers graded as high as Very Good, though Malachi Fields and Isaiah Likely came close. The reason Likely didn't grade higher despite his 9 of 11, 127 yard, 1 TD performance was because of his fumble; PFF grades turnovers very severely. Malik Nabers only had 44 yards on 5 catches in 7 targets, but his very low grade reflects his fumble which cost the Giants a scoring opportunity.

The defense was a different story. There was one excellent defensive performance last night – the defense was not the main reason the Giants lost the game. Ar'Darius Washington has seen his playing time increase the past three weeks, and last night he graded an outstanding 89.9 overall, 91.4 in coverage. Washington was targeted 6 times and gave up 3 completions for a total of only 11 yards and a 16.7 passer rating. He made back-to-back great plays, a pass reception stop and then an interception, with the game on the line and the Commanders in scoring position. His only blemish was 3 missed tackles, but he did have 5 tackles and 2 assists.

The rest of the secondary did not perform as well. Deonte Banks received a barely below average coverage and overall defense grade; if he turns his head and tries to play the ball on the deep pass to Treylon Burks with less than two minutes left in the game, there's no flag and the Giants may end up winning the game. Jevon Holland had the big interception, but he also gave up a TD and 40 yards total on pass plays. Dru Phillips graded 53.5 overall and 48.8 in coverage primarily because he gave up the game-losing TD to Stefon Diggs, but he also missed two tackles. Greg Newsome also graded below average, giving up 22 yards on 2 receptions in 3 targets and missing a tackle. Jason Pinnock graded low as well before leaving the game; he missed 2 tackles and graded low in coverage, primarily because he was covering on the Daniels TD pass to Ben Sinnott.

The Giants' most high profile defensive players (with Brian Burns out for the season) are now Abdul Carter and Arvell Reese. Both were graded Very Good overall by PFF. Reese did nothing in the pass rush last night, but he did have 7 tackles and 5 assists, three of them stops, with no missed tackles. He also gave up four completions in four targets in pass coverage, but they only went for 21 yards total with only 9 YAC, warranting a Very Good coverage grade. Carter is the more difficult evaluation. He continues to be unable to get to the QB for sacks, but he did have a QB hit and 2 hurries plus 2 tackles and an assist, resulting in a Very Good overall defense grade. Micah McFadden had a QB hurry and 9 tackles plus an assist, but he only graded average overall because he surrendered 4 catches and 55 yards on pass defense.

The rest of the defensive line was a bunch of mediocre to poor performances. Kayvon Thibodeaux only had one QB hurry and graded fairly low on run defense despite 7 tackles and an assist, suggesting that he was just blocked out of some running plays. D.J. Reader had his worst game as a Giant, with only 1 hurry and 1 tackle. Shelby Harris got his first sack and had a hurry and 2 tackles, 2 assists. Bobby Jamison-Travis continues to get snaps but had only a tackle and an assist, but that was better than Darius Alexander, who only had one tackle.

The Giants' defense overall can be summed up as: Not bad, but not great either. Here is an overall ranking of NFL defenses in a number of categories:

Overall, the Giants rank No. 12 in the NFL in points per game given up. That sounds pretty good, although you would have to say that other than the Rams, they haven't played any of the NFL's powerhouse offenses. They are below average in sacks, slightly below average in takeaways, and among the league's worst in tackles for loss. It could be a lot worse, but they don't scare anyone either. Speaking of scaring, note that the Jaguars, next week's opponent, are No. 2 in the league in points per game allowed despite not scoring high in any individual category, unlike the No. 1 Vikings, who rank highest in sacks. Somehow the Jags are keeping teams out of the end zone in most of their games. They have the second best red zone D in the NFL, while the Giants' red zone D is near the bottom:

That matters, because as I have noted, John Harbaugh and his offensive coordinator Matt Nagy have mostly been very conservative in their play calling and fourth down decisions. That's quantified below by Kevin Cole of Unexpected Points:

According to the plot above, the Giants go for it on 4th down only about 1 in 3 times when the situation indicates at least a one point win probability added by going for it, one of the lowest rates in the NFL. Here's a related stat about the same philosophy:

At this time it seems appropriate to remind ourselves of the wisdom of Herm Edwards (even though he said this in a different context):

Related to this, here are the NFL rankings for the three-and-out offense rates just over the last three weeks, conveniently coinciding with the J.J. McCarthy as Giants starter era:

Perhaps related to this, here are the checkdown rates for NFL QBs for the past 4 weeks:

So is McCarthy a gunslinger who eschews taking the easy few yards in favor of taking a shot downfield? That might be one way to interpret the deep pass to Nabers that was intercepted on the Giants' last drive, but the question is whether the Nagy offense is giving him an easy answer underneath and J.J. is ignoring or not seeing it vs. giving him no answer at all. On the Nabers INT the pressure up the middle came quickly, but Malachi Fields was right there for McCarthy to throw shallow to and pick up a few yards with plenty of time left for a few more plays:

All that said, in a matchup of one of the league's most highly regarded young QBs against a QB drafted 8 spots lower and jettisoned for a 5th round pick, the performances of Jayden Daniels and J.J. McCarthy weren't all that different last night, and where they were, the difference mostly favored McCarthy:

Daniels had a higher completion rate (69.7% to 63.9%), but J.J.'s CPOE was near zero while Daniels' was -2.1%. McCarthy's ddep throw rate was 11.1% vs. Daniels' 3.0%. The two had almost identical times to throw (2.59 vs. 2.55 s), but McCarthy was pressured 38.5% of the time to Daniels' 20.5%. McCarthy threw into tight windows 11.1% of the time vs. Daniels' 6.1%.

The bottom line is that no one questions Jayden Daniels' ability and status as one of the NFL's best young QBs. McCarthy has traveled a more difficult road. To what extent that is his own fault vs. that of the team that drafted him is a matter for discussion and difficult to ascertain from the outside. But nothing he has shown as a Giant suggests that he cannot be a productive QB at the NFL level. It didn't work out for him last night, but he gave the Giants a chance to win.

#2894. Fantasy Basketball Waiver Wire: 9 NBA Sleepers & Pickups for Yahoo Leagues

Injuries continue to accumulate, meaning that for many fantasy basketball managers, the waiver wire is looking relatively shallow. However, with each new injury, there is a player inching toward a return. Getting ahead of things in fantasy basketball can be crucial when it comes to ROS success. As always, the waiver wire is the place to be, providing managers with bargain players who could be about to ascend the NBA fantasy rankings. And remember, never assume a player is rostered. It always pays to double-check, just in case they have been overlooked.

Identifying players benefiting from expanded roles, whether it's an offensive threat delivering points and threes or a defensive-minded player boosting your blocks and steals, is vital as you navigate the season.

Let's dive into nine key NBA sleepers whose current stats suggest they are poised for significant value and are currently rostered in fewer than 40% of Yahoo leagues.

Larsson has been one of the more underrated, quiet achievers this season, having also recently moved into the starting lineup for Miami. While he doesn't have the highest upside, Larsson is a perfect example of a plug-and-play fantasy asset. Through 11 appearances this season, he has averaged just over 23 fantasy points per game, which puts him in the top-100 range on most nights. Those in High Score leagues can safely deploy him, in the hope that he manages to catch fire from the field or locks down on defense. However, when all else fails, he should still do enough to provide your team with meaningful value.

Johnson continues to work his way back from an early-season ankle injury, which ultimately cost him just three games. Since getting back on the floor, Johnson has played at least 29 minutes in six straight games, scoring at least 18 points in two of his past three appearances. In that three-game period, he has been a borderline top-100 player in High Score leagues, averaging almost 26 fantasy points per contest. Not unlike Larsson, Johnson should be a relatively stable asset, although one with slightly more upside, particularly on the offensive end.

Despite the fact that the landscape in Sacramento is far from predictable, Hunter has managed to carve out a consistent role, logging at least 30 minutes in seven straight games. His overall appeal is built around an ability to score the basketball, which makes him less appealing than both Larsson and Johnson. However, he has recorded at least 20 fantasy points in four of his past six games, with two of those performances yielding 28 and 38 fantasy points. His role feels relatively secure, making him a low-upside target in standard High Score leagues.

Taking a chance on Missi should certainly be considered "getting ahead of the curve," given the ultra-small sample size we have to work with. After a breakout performance in the opening game of the season, Missi suffered a knee injury in game No. 2, which has subsequently cost him 10 straight games. However, it appears he's nearing a return, having been upgraded to doubtful for Friday's game against the Grizzlies. While he could very well remain sidelined for that game, it's reasonable to assume that he could be back as soon as next week. If you are on the hunt for rebounds and blocks, Missi should probably be high on your list of priorities.

We know the drill when it comes to Caruso's fantasy value. As a per-minute guy, he typically delivers for anyone rostering him. However, there are two main issues when it comes to his overall appeal, those being minutes and games played. Typically, the Oklahoma City coaching staff is hesitant to play him more than about 20 minutes on any given night, in an attempt to maximize his games played. With that said, injuries to other players could see Caruso doing a little more than anticipated, especially if his last outing is anything to go by. On the second night of a back-to-back, Caruso finished with 14 points, seven rebounds, three steals and four 3-pointers in 29 minutes. It's pretty simple. Pick him up, cross your fingers and hope for the best.

Known as a steals specialist, Thybulle is one of the more frequent visitors when it comes to the waiver wire. While that fact remains true, Thybulle's role this season feels a little more sustainable than in past seasons. He has played in every game for the Lakers thus far, starting all 13. His playing time has remained relatively stable, allowing him to ease into his new role without the added pressure of trying to "prove himself". He has been a borderline top-100 player to this point, averaging 2.2 steals, 0.8 blocks and 1.7 3-pointers in 24.4 minutes per game. While he is still quite clearly a specialist, a steady role should see him rostered in a lot more leagues than he is currently.

With Stephen Curry set to miss up to a month due to an oblique injury, Melton looks poised to shift into a sizeable role for the foreseeable future. To this point, it's been largely rocks and diamonds when it comes to Melton's fantasy output. Through 12 games, he has averaged 22.5 fantasy points per game, a modest number to be sure. However, assuming his playing time pushes closer to 29 minutes per game, there is scope for him to flirt with 27 or 28 fantasy points, which should see him land inside the top 100 in points formats. If he is available in your league, it makes sense to roll the dice, given his proven ability to contribute across the board.

It's been a solid start to the season for da Silva, having now scored in double digits in six of his past seven games. After being added in most leagues, a recent calf injury saw him dropped in several spots, opening the door for savvy managers to pick him up when the time is right. That time could be now, after da Silva was upgraded to questionable for Friday's game against the Bucks. If he does manage to get back on the floor, there could, of course, be some minor limitations. However, thinking beyond the next one or two games, he is someone who should be picked up in both points and category formats, at least until Paolo Banchero is back on deck.

Thomas continues to go from strength to strength, having carved out a meaningful role for himself in Cleveland. While injuries to other players have certainly helped his cause, there is a chance Thomas has simply moved ahead of certain players, a fact that shouldn't be lost on fantasy managers. He has averaged almost 32 fantasy points per game in his past two appearances, which puts him firmly on the fantasy radar. Assuming he can maintain a 25-minute-per-night role moving forward, he should at least be considered a viable addition in points leagues.

#2887. Cavs at Heat: How to watch, odds, and injury report

The Cleveland Cavaliers are trying to advance out of the group stage of the NBA Cup for the first time. For them to do so, they'll likely need to take advantage of a Miami Heat team that still hasn't found its stride.

Miami has struggled to create any kind of positive momentum since trading for Giannis Antetokounmpo. Their offense hasn't found a correct balance, as they've struggled to get consistent contributions from players outside of Giannis.

Things are worse defensively. They're giving up the fifth-most threes in the league, and opponents are hitting 40% of their outside looks against them (28th). You're just not going to have a good defense if you're struggling to contain the outside shot to that degree.

Cleveland picked up arguably their most impressive win of the season with a strong showing against the New York Knicks. We'll see if they can back up that performance in Miami, even though they'll be without the services of Evan Mobley (toe).

#2881. Did USC football team fly to Indiana before nationwide ground stop?

USC football will need its air attack to be effective against the Indiana Hoosiers, but before the game starts, the Trojans need to get to the game site. You might be wondering if USC's team plane left Los Angeles before the nationwide ground stop ordered by the Federal Aviation Administration on Friday. USA TODAY has updates. So does Chris Vannini of The Athletic.

Vannini reports that USC's plane did leave LA before the ground stop, so USC-Indiana will go on as scheduled on Saturday. Other teams weren't as lucky, and it raises real questions about Saturday's college football schedule. This has become a nationwide story given that the ground stop has nationwide reach. The USC-Indiana game is scheduled for a mid-afternoon kickoff in Bloomington, Indiana, at 3:30 p.m. Eastern time on CBS. That's 12:30 p.m. Pacific time. Had USC not gotten off the ground before the stop, this game might have been subject to a reschedule and a night kickoff to accommodate a later arrival by the visiting team.

Texas did not get off the ground before the stop. The "USC Bowl" between Steve Sarkisian and Lane Kiffin now has another plot point to account for. That game is a scheduled night game, so Horns-Tigers might not be delayed.

Minnesota didn't get out before the ground stop, but the Gophers' game at Penn State is also a night game, so it might not be rescheduled.

This one is interesting. Tennessee did not get out of Knoxville before the ground stop. This game is scheduled for a 3 p.m. Eastern, 2 p.m. local time kickoff on Saturday. This game could get shoved several hours back into a night slot in College Station, Texas.

Ole Miss did not get out of Oxford before the ground stop. This is a 4:15 p.m. Eastern, 3:15 p.m. local time kickoff in Norman, Oklahoma (Central time zone). This game might be pushed back, too.

This is a 1:30 p.m. local kick from Provo in the Mountain time zone. This game could be moved into a night slot. Remember that BYU can't play on Sundays, either, so the Big 12 will want to get this game in by Saturday night.

This is a 3:30 p.m. kickoff in Orlando on Saturday. Arizona State has a long plane flight to deal with, from Tempe (or Phoenix) to Orlando. This game could easily be moved back several hours.

Michigan made it to Oregon. Northwestern made it to Ohio State. Washington did not yet get to East Lansing for the Michigan State game. Purdue will take a bus to get to Iowa City for the game against the Iowa Hawkeyes. Nebraska's plane left before the ground stop and will make it to Rutgers in New Jersey. Illinois plays UCLA Friday night, so the Illini had no problem getting to Los Angeles.

#2880. Cowboys vs. 49ers: Offensive, defensive impact players in Week 10

The Week 10 matchup with the San Francisco 49ers is an opportunity for the Dallas Cowboys to prove they are a better team than the way they have played through the first half of the 2026 NFL season. With the way things have played out thus far, and due to the fact the NFC East division is completely wide-open, Dallas can once again really get into the playoff chase.

It seems far-fetched to believe the Cowboys can still turn things around so much, but that's exactly what's at stake in Week 10. If they can upset the San Francisco 49ers, arguably one of the best teams in the league this year, they could potentially rewrite their season. To do so though the need to make some impact, game-changing plays.

Ryan Flournoy may not have led the Cowboys in receiving yards last week against the Indianapolis Colts, but he did make arguably the most impactful play of the game. With 97 seconds left on the clock in the fourth quarter, QB Dak Prescott heaved the ball downfield hooking up with No. 19, who made a one-handed grab with All-Pro CB Sauce Gardener draped all over him. That reception was pivotal in Dallas regaining the lead.

Like last week, we fully expect No. 19 to make a big impact in the Week 10 matchup with the San Francisco 49ers. With George Pickens limited all week with a hamstring injury, he'll likely be a gametime decision. Even if he does end up playing he probably won't be at 100%. That should give Flournoy ample opportunity to make plays, especially considering the 49ers will focus the majority of their attention on limiting CeeDee Lamb's production.

Caleb Downs is looking to repeat the success he had last week against the Indianapolis Colts in this Week 10 matchup with the San Francisco 49ers. Last week was arguably the best game of his young career. He not only recorded his second QB sack of the season, but also end up sealing the victory for the Cowboys with a game-clinching interception. There's good reason to believe he can be just as impactful in back-to-back weeks.

The 49ers are one of the best offensive units the Cowboys defense has faced up to this point this season. Dallas hasn't had to matchup with a group of talented playmakers thus far this season like they will Sunday afternoon against San Francisco. Due to his versatility to play all over the field, No. 13 should be in the mix of things throughout the ballgame. He'll need to be just as impactful as last week, if not more so, to give Dallas a chance.

Who is your biggest impact player for this week?

#2878. Rockets Marcus Smart suspended three games by NBA

Marcus Smart of the Houston Rockets was suspended yesterday for three games without pay for throwing a punch at Charles Bassey in Wednesday night's victory over the the Golden State Warriors. The incident happened in the third quarter of the Rockets' win, and Smart was ejected and given a flagrant 2.

Smart and Bassey begun arguing after an Amen Thompson drive, and then a Smart right hand connected with Bassey. Houston's head coach Ime Udoka blamed Bassey for running his mouth, however, Bassey claims it was Smart who initiated the incident by "playing dirty the whole game."

"I remember the first half, I was trying to set a screen. He grabbed my knee. He was playing dirty. I think I was trying to get a rebound. He was grabbing my hand, and I was trying to run back on defense. Then, he got up on me and just swung. Obviously, that was weak. I just think that was, honestly, fake toughness.

"It didn't surprise me. I knew he was going to do something. I don't think he was trying to play. I think he was trying to get himself out of the game. I think he was trying to chill. He wasn't trying to hoop. That was fake toughness."

Smart will miss tonight's game against the Utah Jazz, Sunday's game against the Washington Wizards, and Monday's game against the Miami Heat. Reed Sheppard likely picks up extra minutes in Smart's place. He responded on Wednesday with the some clutch play down the stretch. Let's see if that continues.

Smart is averaging 8.3 points, 2.6 rebounds, 2 assists and 1 steal per game for the Rockets while shooting 43.9 percent from the field and 40 percent from deep. Houston is currently at 9-3 and in third place in the Western Conference.

The Rockets will miss Smart, who has somewhat taken on the old Dillon Brooks agitator role, but if any team has the depth to suck it up, it's the Rockets.

#2869. ESPN previews Saturday's massive USC-Indiana matchup

On Saturday, the USC football team will travel on the road to Bloomington to face Indiana. With both the Trojans and Hoosiers still alive in the Big Ten and College Football Playoff races, it is a massive game for both teams.

On Friday, Bill Connelly of ESPN previewed the matchup.

"Indiana fell five spots in the CFP rankings without playing. Curt Cignetti generally doesn't seem to need help motivating his players, but he has an extra card to play in that regard this week.

"Motivation is one thing; the Hoosiers still need better quarterbacking. Josh Hoover's performance fell off a cliff after suffering an only vaguely specified arm injury against Ohio State, and Cignetti needed backup Grantto save the Minnesota game two weeks ago. It appears Hoover will start Saturday, but running back Turbo Richard is out, and the Hoosiers won't defend their national title without better quarterback play.

"They do still have a dynamite defense. IU ranks first in yards allowed per play (4.1), and they're equally good against run and pass. But USC will test the Hoosiers secondary's depth. IU cornerback A.J. Harris is working back from an injury, and though Jamari Sharpe remains an excellent CB No. 1, USC quarterback Jayden Maiava has distributed the ball to a number of targets -- five players have between 21 and 32 catches. USC was shut down by Wisconsin but scored at least 25 points against four other SP+ top-30 defenses. If the Trojans put at least three touchdowns on the board, can IU's offense follow suit?"

Kickoff between USC and Indiana is set for 12:30 p.m. Pacific time/3:30 p.m. Eastern time Saturday on CBS.
