#2807. Yahoo Perfect 5: Victor Wembanyama, Yaxel Lendeborg and De'Anthony Melton lead my Friday lineup

Yahoo Sports is giving fans another way to play fantasy basketball with Perfect 5, a free-to-play game featuring each week's NBA on Prime doubleheader. The rules are simple: pick five players, stay within a $15 budget and rack up fantasy points through scoring, rebounds, assists, defensive stats and 3s.

Friday's slate features the Knicks at Pacers (7 p.m. ET) and Warriors at Spurs (9:30 p.m. ET). The big news came Thursday afternoon: Stephen Curry left the Warriors' 115-108 loss to the Rockets in the first quarter with what turned out to be an oblique tear, and ESPN's Shams Charania reported he's expected to miss close to a month. That knocks out one of the most valuable $5 options on the board and reshuffles the Golden State usage tree, which is exactly where I'm hunting value.

Using Yahoo projections and the latest injury news, I ran 20,000 simulations of the slate across all 542,277 legal $15 lineups. I'm paying up for Victor Wembanyama and stacking the Warriors' backcourt vacuum around him. Here's my lineup:

In my sims, this group averaged 160.24 fantasy points and finished in the top 1% of all possible lineups 35.9% of the time, sixth-best of the more than 542,000 builds I tested. It's 3.07 projected points shy of the max, but it's roughly seven times less likely to be duplicated by the field.

The chalkiest pick in the pool, but why fight it? He's putting up absurd numbers on both sides of the ball. Wembanyama leads the slate at 50.31 projected fantasy points, more than 4.5 clear of anyone else, on the back of 30.3 points, 11.7 rebounds and 4.4 blocks per game. The Spurs have won 10 straight and sit at 10-2. Golden State is now without Curry and has an old and uninspiring frontcourt featuring Draymond Green, Al Horford and Charles Bassey. There's no real size to bother Wemby at the rim.

The one worry is the spread. San Antonio is a 12.5-point favorite, so a blowout could cap his fourth-quarter minutes. Even so, he showed up in the single-best lineup in 33.4% of my sims, and at 81% picked, fading him feels riskier than playing him.

The rookie has been the story of Golden State's start. Lendeborg is averaging 21.7 points, 7.4 rebounds, 3.7 assists and 2.4 3s, and he's firmly in the Rookie of the Year conversation. Against Houston, with Curry leaving after a few minutes, he carried the offense with 25 points, 10 rebounds and two steals in 36 minutes. After the game, Draymond Green told reporters that when Curry and Kevin Durant were off the floor against Houston, he was probably the best player on the court.

At $4, his 41.14 projection is within half a point of Jalen Brunson's at $5. With Curry out, the Warriors have to run their offense through him. He was in the optimal lineup in 32.3% of my sims, third-most of anyone on the slate.

Here's my leverage spot. The math slightly prefers De'Aaron Fox at $3 (33.33 projected), but Fox is picked in 51% of lineups and his team is a double-digit favorite. Melton is projected for 30.26 at the same salary, picked by just 7% and he's the obvious beneficiary of Curry's absence. He's averaging 10.1 points, 4.2 rebounds, 1.3 steals and 2.3 3s, and the steals give him a defensive floor even on a night his shot isn't falling. I won't say he's blowout-proof, but with Brandon Williams also out and no back-to-back to worry about workload, 28-30 minutes is a reasonable expectation.

Melton was in the optimal lineup 25% of the time. Swapping Fox for Melton costs about three projected points, but it's the main reason this build is so much less likely to be duplicated.

Johnson is the best $2 on the board at 23.68 projected fantasy points. He's averaging 8.0 points, 5.7 rebounds and 2.3 assists off the bench. He's also the Spurs player who can benefit if this one gets out of hand, because a blowout means more minutes for the second unit. He was in the optimal lineup in 29% of my sims, the most of any player at $2 or less.

Richard leads every $1 player at 14.68 projected points; the next-best is Harrison Barnes at 11.22. He's averaging 5.5 points and 2.0 rebounds, but Curry's injury pushes more backcourt minutes his way, and he's one of the few $1 options without a DNP-coach's-decision risk. In 25% of my sims, he was in the optimal lineup. He played 19 minutes on Wednesday, finishing with 9 points (3-6 FG, 2-3 3PT, 1-2 FT), 4 boards, a steal and 2 turnovers.

Close. Using Yahoo's projections, the highest-scoring possible lineup is Wembanyama, Lendeborg, Fox, Johnson and Richard at 163.14 fantasy points. Mine is 3.07 points behind it.

The max lineup is the best build on paper. It averaged 163.30 points in my sims and finished in the top 1% 41% of the time, the highest rate of any lineup. The trouble is everyone has it. Wembanyama (81%) and Fox (51%) are the two most popular players in the pool. Based on pick rates, it's about seven times more likely to be duplicated than mine. In a game where you need to finish near the top of the whole Yahoo community, tying a pile of people doesn't help much. Swapping Fox for Melton gives up three projected points, buys a lot of separation and leans into the Curry injury instead of a Spurs starter facing a potential blowout. My lineup still beat the max head-to-head in 42.6% of sims.

If you'd rather keep more of the projection, Wembanyama, Lendeborg, Fox, Landry Shamet and Richard (161.67 projected) was the No. 2 lineup in my sims at a 38.5% top-1% rate. It swaps the 21%-picked Johnson for Shamet at 4%.

If you want to go fully against the grain, the best build without Wembanyama is Karl-Anthony Towns, Lendeborg, Fox, Johnson and Richard.

Towns is picked in just 7% of lineups, yet he's the second-highest projection on the slate thanks to 20.9 points and 13.6 rebounds per game. New York is a 7.5-point road favorite. The build averaged 158.73 points and finished in the top 1% 32.8% of the time. If Wembanyama sits late in a Spurs blowout, this lineup jumps past most of the field.

For a deeper fade, Lendeborg, Brandin Podziemski, Fox, Johnson and Shamet (158.58 projected, 32.7% top-1% rate) skip the $5 tier entirely. It doubles up on the Warriors' top two healthy scorers, and Podziemski is picked in just 2% of lineups while averaging a career-high 17.3 points.

You can start building your Perfect 5 roster for Friday's slate now, including going up against me in my Perfect 5 group. Good luck!

#2806. What's ahead for AI financing after Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang departs

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's departure hasn't produced a broad shock to the credit market, but it is putting the company's role as a financier of artificial intelligence infrastructure under scrutiny.

Huang, who stepped down Thursday amid an espionage and adultery scandal, built Nvidia into an AI powerhouse in the 33 years since he co-founded the company. Chief financial officer Colette Kress has been named interim CEO as the company searches for a permanent chief executive.

Nvidia not only supplies its graphics processing units to hyperscalers and tech giants, it also provides financing to other companies to help the industry expand. That could all change.

"Although we expect the company to fulfill all of its previously announced commitments, we believe that going forward, it may be more difficult for the company to negotiate new multi-party investments among ecosystem partners at the same scale it has done up to this point," Goldman Sachs analyst James Schneider said in a note Thursday.

The news of Huang's resignation comes after investors were already concerned about the massive amount of debt being taken on to fund AI infrastructure.

Tech bond yields have risen and spreads have widened on credit default swaps, most notably in lower-rated names. Credit default swaps are contracts in which investors pay for protection in case the borrower can't repay its debt. Wider spreads indicate investors view the bonds as riskier.

On Friday, the yield on Nvidia's 4.5% bond due in 2031 sat at 5.717%, nearly 5% higher week to date. Bond yields move inversely to prices.

Tech stocks slumped Friday with the Nasdaq 100 closing down 1.2% as markets continued to digest the Nvidia news. Yields on hyperscaler bonds ticked slightly higher on Friday, signaling the broader concern about AI debt lingers.

It's the smaller AI companies that could ultimately see an impact if Nvidia makes any changes to its AI financing. The company had $36 billion in off-balance-sheet AI cloud agreements, or backstops, for neoclouds, according to its last quarterly financial filing in July.

Neoclouds CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN are all in Nvidia's portfolio. Their shares were down sharply Thursday.

CoreWeave, which has become the poster child for AI debt fears, saw its bond yields retreat after its quarterly earnings on Tuesday eased investor concerns. The yield on its 9.75% bond due in 2031 was 14.79% on Friday after climbing to 16.7% last week. The spread on its five-year CDS also dropped more than 17% week to date, although it remained above 1,000 — which is generally considered a sign of distress.

It remains unclear how Nvidia's support for those companies may change, said CreditSights analyst Andy Li.

"We don't know what the ultimate strategy for Nvidia is going to be coming out the other side," he said.

"However, I think directionally, just given the fact that Jensen was so crucial in selling the Nvidia story and being a very aggressive deal maker, it's likely that a lot of this creative deal-making and circular financing and equity and credit support is going to moderate," he added.

CoreWeave CEO Michael Intrator told CNBC Wednesday, before Huang's departure, that Nvidia "is not one person."

"There are thousands of people over there that interface with thousands of people at my company every day. They integrate on the technology. They integrate on the physical infrastructure. They integrate on the networking," he said in an interview with "Squawk on the Street." "They have been a great partner for us for years. They will continue to be a great partner for us."

Bob Lang, founder and chief strategist at Explosive Options, doesn't see any big changes in terms of Nvidia's relevance or its ability to finance for now. As CFO, Kress was involved in the financing deals, and the search for a permanent CEO is going to take some time, he noted.

The real issue is just how much money the company will want to lend for AI financing, rather than, say using its hundreds of billions in free cash flow for other purposes, like share buybacks, capex, or acquisitions.

"Big companies like Nvidia and Meta, they're holding all the cards," he said. "When they decide that the game is over, that's it — it's going to be curtains for some of these smaller companies."

— CNBC'S Kif Leswing and Isabel O'Brien contributed reporting.

#2805. The Steelers can’t stop talking about this practice squad WR

It has been a busy few weeks of Steelers wide receiver discourse. There have been trade rumors about Josh Downs, Brian Thomas Jr., and Calvin Ridley. There's been talk about when Roman Wilson and Michael Pittman Jr. can return off of injured reserve. And hey, did you know Brandon Aiyuk is set to be available soon?

But amid all the rumors, the Steelers can't seem to stop talking about one potential addition to the room: Max Hurleman, who is currently on practice squad injured reserve.

"I mean, how do you not love Max (Hurleman)?" Steelers head coach Mike McCarthy said Thursday. "As a new coach, new coaching staﬀ, you come in here every day doing everything right. GPS (data), (he) burned it up. I mean, the guy has an incredible workload capacity, work ethic."

McCarthy said it was unfortunate that Hurleman was injured early in the season, with the receiver landing on practice squad injured reserve on Sept. 19 with what Steelers beat reporter Mark Kaboly recently described as an "undisclosed but suspected leg injury."

"I felt that (Hurleman) truly performed to a level that he deserved a 53-man roster spot," McCarthy said. "So, it'll be great to get him back and get him back into the fold."

It appears Hurleman could be getting close to a return. On Wednesday, quarterback Aaron Rodgers mentioned the wide receiver when asked about the Steelers getting healthier at the position.

"We've obviously raised up a couple of our P-Squad guys who are talented. Still would love to get someopportunities for Max (Hurleman) at some point whenever he comes oﬀ," Rodgers told the media. "We have Marquez (Valdes-Scantling), who hasn't been brought up yet, so we have a lot of options. But I'm hopeful that we'll get (Michael Pittman Jr.) and Roman (Wilson) back sooner rather than later."

Also on Wednesday, McCarthy brought up Hurleman in the same breath as Pittman and Wilson. "We've got a group that I'm excited to continue to work with," the Steelers coach said.

Steelers WR1 DK Metcalf also seems excited for the return of Hurleman, coining the nickname "White Chocolate" for the second-year receiver.

"White Chocolate gonna get y'all some plays, especially when he's out there on the field, so I'm looking forward to him making his debut," Metcalf told the media on Thursday. "(Hurleman) has been making plays since his rookie year last year, so definitely looking forward to him being out there."

Hurleman has yet to play in an NFL regular season game.

"It's very humbling whenever your name gets brought up," Hurleman said, per Kaboly. "I've been working my tail off every day to come back. To get the recognition, that means a lot, but obviously, there's a ton of work to do. Haven't played a game yet, but it's awesome to hear they trust me and see me as an option."

Hurleman initially joined the Steelers as an undrafted free agent out of Notre Dame in 2025. He finished his college career in South Bend after four years at Colgate, entering the NFL with experience at running back, wide receiver, cornerback and returner. He's largely settled into a wide receiver role with the Steelers, where he's made some highlight plays in recent preseasons.

The 5'11, 200-pound receiver still hasn't been activated off of practice squad injured reserve, but it appears that the Steelers have some plans for Hurleman once he returns.

Patriots' Milton Williams institutes a $1,000 fine for New England defenders who jump offsides appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

#2803. Is a bottom in for the Nasdaq-100? Charts to watch

(See the video above for Frank's complete chart breakdown.)

Is the Nasdaq-100's recent bounce a sign that the bottom is in? We're going to look at several charts to help answer that question.

First, let's remember how we got here. In September, the NDX broke out from a bullish cup-and-handle pattern, producing a 33,200-upside target. That breakout quickly failed, however, and gave way to a bearish pattern that broke down aggressively two weeks ago.

The decline brought the index relatively close to its 26,350 downside target before the current rebound began.

Zooming out, it's telling to see where the bounce originated. Three important forms of support converged in the 25,000-26,000 area:

This confluence of support helped encourage buying after a roughly 15% decline.

For perspective, we also see what larger drawdowns of 20%, 30%, 40%, and 50% would look like … a lot worse.

The following chart tracks NDX drawdowns from 52-week highs going back to just after the financial crisis. Only a handful of declines exceeded 20%, and three produced relatively quick reversals: late 2018, the Covid lows in 2020 and April 2025.

Historically, buying into declines of approximately 15%–25% has generally paid off, even when additional downside occurred first (yellow highlights).

The chart also highlights previous declines of 10%–16% in green, putting the current pullback into perspective.

The major exception was 2022, when the NDX eventually declined nearly 36% over approximately 10 months ... a much worse outcome, which many investors are concerned could happen again now.

That raises the key question: Are we experiencing another routine correction, or the early stages of a larger bear market?

One important clue will be how far this bounce progresses.

Any rally that fails to reclaim the previous highs could establish a significant lower high. Additional lower highs would provide stronger evidence of a developing, longer downtrend.

We saw this in March 2022, when that first sharp rebound effort failed to reach the previous high before a much more severe decline followed.

The bottom line: The current bounce is encouraging, particularly given where it originated and knowing how well buying prior 10-20% drawdowns has paid off.

From here, it's all about the follow through.  We don't need to see new highs right away, but any rally from here that's met with another wave of strong selling could reignite downside momentum.

Frank Cappelleri is the founder and president of CappThesis, an independent research firm that helps active investors through time-tested chart and statistical analysis. Prior to starting CappThesis, he spent 25 years on Wall Street as an equity sales trader, technical analyst, research sales specialist and desk strategist.

#2802. How real are the Lakers, Spurs and the 10 teams slugging it out in the West?

The Western Conference is tough, a slugfest, a large predicament. None of that is new information. There was, however, a stat from last week that stopped me in my tracks. It was the moment where the West's top-five teams were all on winning streaks of at least four games for the first time since conferences began.

All of the current top 10 teams in the West have won at least six of their past 10 games. All of the current top-five are on a winning streak. Six of the current top-10 teams have won their last game. The four teams that have lost? It's because they had to play another top Western Conference team.

According to the NBA, eight of the top-10 teams in offensive rating are in the West, six of the top-10 teams in defensive rating are in the West, and seven of the top-10 teams in net rating are in the West.

It is still early in the process of the regular season. These teams are still learning who they are on this journey. It is also very real that early success can lead to even more buy-in, which can sustain a team over the course of a season. How real are these early-season runs from the Western Conference? Let's take a look at each team.

Yes, Luka Dončić is still on a preposterous run to start the season. Yes, Austin Reaves (who suffered a heel injury on Wednesday) is having a career. And, yes, the Lakers currently have the best offense in basketball. What could make this stick, though, is the belief this team has and the effort it's given on defense.

There is an activity the Lakers have brought to the defensive end of the floor. It's not particularly new from what they've attempted to do in the past, but the execution appears more consistent throughout the roster. Switching on- and off-ball actions, working to contain drives and showing early help have been hallmarks of the Lakers' defense.

Luka Dončić and the Lakers have reason to smile. (AP Photo/Justine Willard)

Walker Kessler's impact has been felt with his work to not just protect the rim, but to consistently rotate to get in position. There's a reason he leads the league in total contested shots (150, 11.5 per game). Whether it's from the baseline, elbow or wing, Kessler's timing has been key in the Lakers' ability to protect the paint (opponents average 43.1 points in the paint, fourth-lowest in league).

When the Lakers trend smaller with Kessler off the court, the switching becomes more constant and the attempts at activity ramp up, with multiple lineups that can consistently hit those notes. They'll be tested by switching on both ends eventually, but it feels like the vision and the formula are in alignment.

If there were any questions about the Spurs' ability to rise back to the top of the Western Conference after a wobbly 0-2 start … I believe 10 straight wins provides an answer. The shift to the three-guard lineup of De'Aaron Fox, Stephon Castle and Dylan Harper has had its bumpy moments, but appears to be turning a corner.

We are starting to feel the moments where a defense is in rotation and all three can attack. That shift also does not take away from the fact the Spurs still can have 48 minutes of quality guard play throughout a game. This opens up lineup versatility (which will be huge come playoff time) and has contributed to the Spurs' overall offensive balance.

It also helps that Victor Wembanyama has gotten even better on both ends. In his last five games, he's averaging 36.6 points, 13 rebounds, and 5 blocks in 28.4 minutes a night, while shooting 65.6% from the field and 41.4% from 3. His physicality is showing up in different ways: working to catch in his spots; being in position to set screens lower on the floor; releasing to his rolls quicker. It'll be hard to doubt the Spurs if the execution continues to level up.

Minnesota had shown the formula and potency of its starting lineup to start this season. That the Timberwolves have been able to expand it while Anthony Edwards has been on the shelf speaks volumes. If the question is where exactly would LaMelo Ball elevate the Wolves, the answer has been everywhere.

Ball's playmaking has enhanced their transition and pick-and-roll attack. The shotmaking and drives have added another layer. Jonathan Kuminga has continued to showcase the value of being wanted by doing exactly what the Wolves have needed him to do. Jaden McDaniels and Ayo Dosunmu remain aggressive. What boosts this attack is the fact that the strong defense has held up: activity, ball pressure, off-ball stunts to disrupt. Size and length to switch and bother with Rudy Gobert roaming in the paint can cause chaos. It's hard to imagine this team will not continue to build on its emerging confidence and chemistry.

It's the combination of a reliable, strong defense and a commitment to evolving their offense that has helped the Rockets ascend to start this season. Their best moments have come when they have gotten the ball up quick, gotten to multiple actions and kept things moving.

Kevin Durant is fully scoring within the flow of their offense, meaning you have to defend multiple things and then Kevin Durant gets the ball. Fred VanVleet has steadied the ship, mixing in pull-up 3s while reading defenses to make plays (without turning the ball over). It's not just that all of their young players are able to "slide down a slot" — their roles are more concise, allowing for more consistency and confidence. The challenge is, will they revert to old habits if either the defense or offense slides backward?

A hallmark of this Blazers start has been a collective commitment to buying into their roles and working to do the little things every single night. Damian Lillard's return has been riddled of reminders of the talent he brings to the table. The logo 3s are one thing, the ability to mix in a timely drive is another, and he's willing to put the work in on the defensive end.

Toumani Camara will attempt to guard everyone in an opposing jersey. The emergence of Sidy Cissoko means the Blazers have a consistency of guard, wing and big play throughout their lineups. It's less about if they can do it, more of a matter of how many times can they repeat it. No matter where they land in the standings, you'll have to deal with the Blazers all year long. Will they be ready for the success that comes with it?

Well, the test for Golden State now is how real can this run be with Stephen Curry out for an extended amount of time? Curry was tremendous to start the season, but the Warriors were combining his excellent play with working to evolve offensively. More pick-and-roll (with more cutting around it), more willingness to attack matchups, mixing in their traditional splits vs. leaning on it as a base.

Yaxel Lenderborg has been a revelation, continuing to gain trust with each performance. Now we'll see just how strong that base is and if the defense that has impressed early can hold up in a scenario where they will have to get stops. It's always been about if the Warriors can win enough regular-season games to get the true payoff in the playoffs.

An unfortunate amount of injuries has led to the Thunder having to work through a different kind of adversity early in the season. If there is one thing about this team, it's that it's always believed in internal growth and optionality. The Thunder have always worked to stay consistent with their approach, and that's what may be the thing that helps lift them through this season.

Shai Gilgeous-Alexander being moved off-ball can make him more sudden in a universe where teams may throw more attention his way. There's plenty of opportunity for Jared McCain and Ajay Mitchell to make an impact offensively. But can they grind out enough offense over the course of the season? If nothing else this team will have to defend at an elite level, and if the defense sticks, the Thunder will be no fun to deal with.

What a contrast of two months as the Grizzlies went 6-0 in October and have gone 1-4 as of this writing in November. And all four of those losses came in the Grindhouse; home losses are the most consistent regret a Western Conference team can have.

There's a reason for their early success, though: It's been a team effort on both ends. Cameron Boozer has helped to elevate what they want to get to offensively. Ty Jerome has been game to take, make, decel and stepback his way to victory. There's a sneaky depth to this team that can be felt from game to game. The big thing is seeing how they adjust as more scouting reports and game plans come in. Taking the league by surprise is one thing, but how will they respond once they've earned a level of respect?

This is not the start the Nuggets had in mind, but the building blocks have not disappeared. On the season, Jamal Murray is averaging 26.2 points on 50.9% shooting from the field and 51.3% from 3. Nikola Jokić is still Nikola Jokić despite feelings he has fallen off. Cam Johnson has stayed aggressive, DeMar DeRozan adds a different element. The lineup versatility unlocked by the play of Spencer Jones and Trevon Brazile should pay off down the line.

The biggest question is, can they get enough out of their defense and can their offense still deliver the fastball? We know how tough Jokić/Murray/Aaron Gordon can be to defend, but if opponents find a consistent comfort, how much does that change the Nuggets' winning formula?

Cooper Flagg's start to the season is going to bring him and this Mavericks team into conversations that may not have been expected this soon. The team's build is what has intrigued me. Improving on both ends of the floor is not easy, and Dallas has risen from 27th in offensive rating and 19th in defensive rating last season to 12th in offensive rating and ninth in defensive rating. That is something.

Dusty May has built in principles the Mavericks have worked to hit. An offense that enhances what Flagg brings to the table, takes nothing away from Kyrie Irving and also empowers the rest of the roster is a positive. Defensively, they have several lineups that can have a lot of size and switchability, and they tend to be a bend-don't-break type of team at their peak. Drives are hard to come by, shots are contested in the paint, you just have to deal with them. As the season progresses, they will have to navigate learning how to win and finding a way to do what they do well consistently.

#2801. Bruce Arians says he wants to be a head coach in the NFL next season

Bruce Arians is thinking about coming out of retirement. Again.

Arians told Jordan Schultz that when he announced his retirement from the Buccaneers in March of 2022, it was because of health problems that have since been resolved, and he now wants to coach again.

“I had torn my Achilles early in that season, and I was wearing these prosthetic high heels, and it threw my back out, and I couldn’t stand on the sideline for four hours anymore without a lot of pain,” Arians said. “I was also monitoring an aneurysm on my heart, so I had some health issues. We did everything we thought we could do in Tampa after winning the Super Bowl, trying to get two, and then it was time to give it to Todd [Bowles]. Now that I’m all healthy again, I got the itch again. So, we’ll see.”

If Arians does come back next year, he’ll be the oldest head coach in NFL history. That distinction currently belongs to Pete Carroll, who was 74 when he coached the Raiders last season. Arians will turn 75 next season.

Arians’ first retirement announcement came from the Steelers after they did not renew his contract as their offensive coordinator following the 2011 season, but Arians later said he was forced out and didn’t want to retire. He then took a job as offensive coordinator of the Colts in 2012, and was their interim head coach for most of that season while Chuck Pagano battled leukemia. Arians coached so well that he was named the NFL’s Coach of the Year.

Once Pagano returned to the Colts, Arians became head coach of the Cardinals, and he won a second Coach of the Year award. He announced his retirement following the 2017 season, after five years as head coach of the Cardinals.

But in 2019 he came out of retirement to become head coach of the Buccaneers. He won a Super Bowl in Tampa Bay, then retired after three seasons. Arians thought that was it, but he said that his son is now coaching high school football, and in talking to his son about the job he realized how much he misses it himself.

“Another fire got lit,” Arians said. “I had no desire to go back, and I was still fighting some health issues, but now after the open heart surgery I’ve got so much more energy, it’s amazing.”

Arians may find, like Bill Belichick before him, that all the accomplishments in the world don’t guarantee an NFL head-coaching job. Or perhaps he’ll convince some owner to give the oldest coach ever a chance to do what he’s done three times before, and turn a franchise around.

#2800. A trading strategy to ride out the Nvidia uncertainty and generate income in the process

Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation has sent shockwaves through the tech sector, dragging Nvidia's (NVDA) stock down roughly 30% since Oct. 6. The market despises uncertainty, and losing a visionary founder, particularly one without a well-telegraphed succession plan, makes it worse.

However, for opportunistic investors, this panic has made the stock cheaper (obviously) while options have become significantly more expensive. Trading in the $160s, Nvidia's valuation has taken a big haircut, yet its underlying business fundamentals remain largely intact. While Huang was the architect of Nvidia's AI dominance, the company's moat — superior GPUs, the CUDA software ecosystem, and networking expertise — does not walk out the door with him. The company's structural advantage in the global artificial intelligence rollout persists.

Buying good companies at a discount is investing 101, but the current market dynamics may do something else for those who use options.  Huang's departure guarantees that Nvidia will be operating under a cloud of transition for the foreseeable future. Executing a permanent CEO search while navigating global regulatory scrutiny takes time. Consequently, Nvidia's implied volatility (IV)—the market's pricing of expected future price swings — has spiked to highs not seen since the tariff tantrum in the first half of 2025, and this elevated IV is highly likely to persist for several months as the leadership drama slowly unfolds.

This prolonged period of high implied volatility can be a goldmine for option sellers, specifically those executing options overlays such as covered call strategies. When you buy NVDA shares at these depressed levels and simultaneously sell out-of-the-money call options against your position, the premiums you collect are directly tied to IV. Because fear and transition-related uncertainty have dramatically inflated these option prices, the income generated from selling covered calls is now significantly higher than usual.

This lowers the risk profile of holding the stock considerably over time. Consistently collecting these inflated premiums systematically lowers your overall cost basis in the underlying shares. If the stock trades sideways or recovers slowly during the CEO search, you are generating an outsized cash yield on a stagnant asset. If the stock experiences further downside turbulence, the heavy premium income acts as a substantial downside buffer, meaningfully reducing the strategy's overall risk over time.

By deploying a persistent covered call strategy, investors can leverage the exact fear driving the stock down to generate robust, risk-reducing income while maintaining exposure to the undeniable future of AI.

#2797. Why the Cowboys matchup could be difficult for the 49ers this weekend

The San Francisco 49ers are hitting the road for a storied rivalry game against the Dallas Cowboys this weekend, as they look to improve to 8-1 after a blowout win over the Las Vegas Raiders.

San Francisco entered the week as 1.5-point underdogs, but the lines have shifted drastically, with the 49ers now being 3.5-point favorites ahead of game day. The Cowboys do have several players injured, including cornerback Joey Porter Jr., but it looks like they'll have star receiver George Pickens back after he started the week with two limited practices.

Meanwhile, San Francisco could be without Deommodore Lenoir, Mike Evans, and Renardo Green, as the trio did not practice at all on Wednesday or Thursday. Evans was expected to miss another week, but losing both cornerbacks is a big deal, especially against a Cowboys offense looking to throw the ball.

So, it's not a surprise that the game total is sky-high at 55.5 points, with oddsmakers expecting fireworks from both offenses.

San Francisco did re-sign cornerback Jack Jones to the practice squad this week after releasing him a few weeks ago. While Jones had some positive moments this season, he had really struggled before getting cut by the 49ers, and it wasn't clear that he was going to return. But with the injuries, he could be called upon to play quickly.

"I don't think there's ever an understanding he's coming back," Morris said of re-signing Jones. "This league is, we know this league, we've all been in it long enough, covered it long enough, done whatever, but I do think Jack Jones handled himself the right way. He was accountable to the things that he did wrong. I was accountable to the things that I could help him out to do better, so when he left, he left on great terms. When you leave a place on great terms, you always have an opportunity to come back when something presents itself.

"So last week, an opportunity presented itself and I think him coming back with a different vibe about himself and a different perspective gives him the best chance. I think that's the only way you can handle failure and the only way to get improvement is to have a little bit of failure. I look forward to what he can do and how he can help us if it's possible and got a lot of confidence in what he was able to do before that too as well."

Well, Jones will have to handle that failure quickly, as he gets a very tough matchup this weekend going up against CeeDee Lamb and George Pickens. The 49ers defense has graded out well by a number of metrics this season, but they are now banged up in the secondary with Deommodore Lenoir and Renardo Green's statuses in question.

That's why this matchup against the Cowboys could be a tougher one than expected for the 49ers. And it gets tougher from here, as the 49ers play the Vikings, Seahawks, Giants, and Rams over their next four games. This five-game stretch will tell us a lot about San Francisco, which remains the top contender to make the Super Bowl from the NFC.

Starting that stretch off with a win would be huge, but that means some players from the secondary, be it Jones or Nate Hobbs or even Ephesians Prysock, will have to step up on Sunday.

#2791. Yankees Target Alec Bohm As Skubal Talks Heat Up

The New York Yankees are looking hard at third base options as the winter approaches. Reports indicate the club has interest in Philadelphia Phillies star Alec Bohm. This move aims to fix a position that has struggled for years. The team needs a steady hand at the hot corner to support the lineup. While the interest is real, some reports suggest the depth of that interest is still unclear.

General Manager meetings in Las Vegas are currently underway. These talks happen while a lockout looms on December 1. Scott Boras, the agent for ace pitcher Tarik Skubal, says the market is strong. He told reporters that teams know there is only one Tarik card available. The Yankees are also talking to catcher Carson Kelly. These moves show the front office is ready to act before the winter freeze.

Ryan McMahon was brought in to play third base in 2025. His defense showed some issues last season with -3 DRS at the position. The group that played there combined for a .629 OPS. That number was the fifth-worst mark in the entire league. The farm system does not have many good infielders waiting in the wings. Dax Kilby is a top prospect, but the team needs help now. Bohm could offer a solution by platooning with McMahon. He hits right-handed while McMahon hits left-handed.

Bohm has spent his whole career with the Phillies. He hit 15 homers and drove in 88 runs last year. His career batting average sits at .273 with a .735 OPS. He does not strike out much, with a rate well below the league average. Per MLB.com, he crushed left-handed pitching with an .853 OPS against them. However, his defense has been a bit below average throughout his time in the majors. He posted a career-worst Fielding Run Value of -9 in 2026.

Tarik Skubal has booked more meetings as the agent market heats up. His stats from 2026 were impressive with a 2.72 ERA and 186 strikeouts. Boras noted that the anticipated offseason was a bearish begin. It is now evident, according to him, that a powerful and dominant Sku-bull market exists. This shows that teams are ready to pay for top-tier pitching. The Yankees have a strong need for an ace to lead the rotation. The window to sign him is open before the league shuts down.

Jon Heyman of the New York Post first reported the interest in Bohm. However, other sources say the Yankees have not shown much interest to this point. The club has not officially confirmed any deal or deep talks. A source suggested the team is just looking at the possibilities. The hot corner will probably become a priority as this offseason unfolds. The Yankees must decide if they make a big move or wait.

Brendan Kuty indicates the club might display interest at a later time. General Manager Brian Cashman admitted they need to improve production in many spots. "He's signed through next year for us, but I definitely know we need to improve production in a lot of different spots on the club if we can," he told reporters on Tuesday. The team will be open to deals if they find the right fit.

The Major League Baseball offseason for 2026-27, which remains uncertain, has fully begun. A lockout is expected to start on December 1. The Red Sox are also shopping outfielder Jarren Duran. Boston has shown interest in free agent Seiya Suzuki as well. The market for top talent remains active despite the looming work stoppage. According to CBS Sports, no Winter Meetings might occur during this offseason.

#2790. The Lakers’ schedule will give them runway into big early-season tests

The Lakers are riding a seven-game win streak and not only sit atop the league with an 11-2 record, they were the first team in the league to play 13 games in this early season. What looked like an easy schedule based on strength of opponent was countered by a rigorous slate that included three back-to-backs and multiple stretches of three games in four nights and five games in seven nights.

Los Angeles' schedule wasn't nearly as easy as people make it out to be, as referenced by the physical toll it's already taken on many of its most prominent players, including Luka Dončić and Austin Reaves, who each made it out of the first extended road trip of the season battered up.

Between the back-to-backs and surplus of games in a short span, the Lakers came out of this early slate with a lot more answers than questions. There were wins against quality teams like Detroit, Atlanta, Portland and Toronto and survival in potential trap games without one of Dončić or Reaves.

"The biggest takeaway is that this is a great group of guys who really enjoy playing together and really enjoy playing for each other," Redick said of their first road trip. "But we got a lot of work to do and we got to keep getting better."

The Lakers withstood everything the schedule makers threw their way. And for that, they'll be rewarded with a less demanding runway of games that can serve as another ramp-up before some of their biggest tests of the season.

Before taking on Phoenix on Sunday, the Lakers are enjoying their first three-day break of the season, which gave the team time to travel back from Milwaukee and actually practice in El Segundo on Friday. The Suns are 5-6 and coming off a much-needed win against Charlotte. Their early season outlook hasn't been the best, but they're among the more talented under-.500 teams the Lakers have played so far.

Once the Lakers return from Arizona, they have four games at home over seven days against the Hornets (4-8), Utah twice (1-11) and Sacramento (1-10). When you include the Suns, Los Angeles's opponents up until Thanksgiving have a combined record of 11-35.

This is a crucial stretch for the Lakers to string together some more easy early-season wins while ramping up for a gauntlet to end November, a three-game slate against San Antonio, Memphis and Houston. The Spurs are just half a game behind L.A. in the standings, while the Rockets sit 1.5 games behind and the Grizzlies just three games behind.

According to ESPN's Relative Percent Index (RPI), a tool used to calculate a team's strength of schedule and, in turn, a power ranking based on said schedule, the Lakers rank fifth in RPI. Houston is first, San Antonio is sixth and Memphis is 17th. The RPI also states that the Lakers have had the 15th-toughest schedule thus far.

Not only is that three-game stretch to end the month against three of the best teams the Lakers will have played so far, it's also three games in four nights, on the road, starting the day after Thanksgiving, including a back-to-back in Memphis and Houston.

If they take care of business over these next five games as they have so far this season, the Lakers could enter that slate with a staggering 16-2 record for the first time since the 2019-20 championship season and for fifth time in franchise history, according to Stathead.

We're about to see just how serious a team they are.

#2789. Troy Aikman engages in potential pre-tampering with Kyle Shanahan

If Troy Aikman exits broadcasting after this season (last month, he put the chance at “50-50”) and if Aikman lands a key position in a front office, the name at the top of his head-coaching wishlist will already be known.

Via Brandon Contes of Awful Announcing, Aikman said during a Thursday appearance on 96.7 The Ticket in Dallas that, if he were running an NFL team, he would try to hire 49ers coach Kyle Shanahan.

“I’ve been asked a lot of questions here, certainly over the last several weeks, about any aspirations that I may have of going in a front office,” Aikman said. “Or ‘Who would you hire as your head coach?’ If I was in a position to hire someone as a head coach, [the] first call I’d make is to the 49ers and say, ‘How much is it going to take to get Kyle Shanahan? Because I think he’s that good. And yes, I’ve always believed this, but coaching matters and it has an impact on an organization.”

It’s not tampering because Aikman currently isn’t running a team. (It could technically be “tampering light” if he’s still working for the Dolphins.) And the information gives Shanahan plenty of leverage in his dealings with the 49ers, if Aikman joins the likes of Matt Millen, John Lynch, and John Elway as former players with little or no front-office experience who find themselves working in high-level jobs with a team.

Aikman has previously made similar comments about Shanahan, in the context of the Bears’ latest coaching vacancy.

“There has been discussion about, ‘Do you give up some picks and see if you can maybe make a deal for a guy like Kyle Shanahan?’” Aikman said during a game in 2024. “I would be all in on that. Because he would develop a quarterback, and he’s a hell of a head coach. But can you pull that off? That’s a better question. And who might you hire that has no head-coaching experience as an offensive guy? You never know the answer to that, as to how good they’re gonna be.”

While it seems to be working out pretty well for the Bears and their head coach who had no head-coaching experience, the point remains that Aikman would try to hire Shanahan, if Aikman is ever in a position to hire a coach.

And there’s one last twist to this story that currently isn’t known. When Aikman was consulting with the Dolphins during their latest search for a G.M. and head coach, did Aikman advise Miami to call about Shanahan? If so, did they?

Shanahan is in his 10th season with the 49ers. He’s taken the team to two Super Bowls. Barring the franchise’s first championship since 1994 (which could come this year), there could be a point where Shanahan reaches an Andy Reid-style mutual expiration date.

If that ever happens, Shanahan will have options. Especially if Aikman ends up getting hired to be someone’s G.M.

#2787. The Packers’ Defense Is Living on Takeaways

Nine games into 2026, the Green Bay Packers' defense has 19 takeaways: 13 interceptions and 6 fumble recoveries. Last season, they had 14 in 17 games. Sunday's 45-27 win in Foxborough made it six straight games with multiple takeaways. Green Bay took the ball away four times and turned those turnovers into 21 points.

What stands out is that it isn't coming from blitzing. Against New England, Green Bay never rushed more than four, and all three interceptions came with Drake Maye in a clean pocket. The Packers are winning after the snap, with disguise, rotation and safeties who read the quarterback.

Safeties Xavier McKinney and Evan Williams have nine takeaways between them, almost half the team's total.

McKinney leads the team with five interceptions, including two-pick games against Chicago and New England. On Sunday, he picked off Maye's first pass of the game and returned it to the New England 14. Later he read Maye's eyes from a deep-quarter alignment and drove on a backside dig.

Williams has three interceptions and a fumble recovery. His pick against the Patriots came off a Brandon Cisse deflection on a deep shot, with Williams rotating over the top while McKinney was used as a rusher.

Nine different defenders have a takeaway. Six of them have an interception. Isaiah McDuffie had the best all-around day in Week 9. He stepped up when Ty'Ron Hopper left with a chest injury and became the first Packer since 2000 with multiple tackles for loss, a sack, a forced fumble and a fumble recovery in one game. His strip-and-recover of Rhamondre Stevenson in the third quarter ended any thought of a Patriots comeback.

McDuffie also has two forced fumbles. Javon Bullard, Zaire Franklin, Devonte Wyatt and Anthony Campbell have one each.

Green Bay has done this without its biggest name. The defense has played all nine games without All-Pro edge rusher Micah Parsons. It has also lost linebackers Edgerrin Cooper and Zaire Franklin, and three defensive tackles, to injured reserve. A defense that can't count on pressure has had to take the ball away with coverage instead. So far it has worked: the Packers are 6-3 and have won five of their last six.

Takeaway rates swing a lot from year to year, and some regression should be expected. Still, a defense whose turnovers come from disguise and safety play has a sturdier base than one living on tipped balls.

#2782. Cam Skattebo stayed in his stance instead of pushing Jameis Winston's tush on key play

Giants running back Cam Skattebo stayed in his stance and didn’t participate in the play on a key short-yardage snap late in the Giants’ loss on Thursday night.

With the Giants leading 21-17 and 3:56 remaining in the fourth quarter on Thursday night, they faced a third-and-1 and sent Jameis Winston in for a quarterback sneak. A first down would have given the Giants a great chance at a long, clock-draining drive. Instead they were stopped, punted, and ended up losing.

As Winston plowed into the line, Skattebo was behind him and could have run in to try to push him forward to gain a yard. Instead, Skattebo stayed in his stance, and by the time he actually got involved in the play, the Commanders had stopped Winston’s forward progress.

It’s unclear why Skattebo didn’t do anything, and he wasn’t asked about it after the game. But at a time when every team in the league is well aware of the value of teammates pushing the quarterback forward on sneaks, it was a bizarre play, and one that proved costly for the Giants.

#2774. Chiefs-Falcons Week 10 game and score predictions from Arrowhead Pride

It's time for the Arrowhead Pride staff to put their opinions to the test. Before the Kansas City Chiefs travel to face the Atlanta Falcons, our writers predicted the final score and result.

Atlanta has been an unpredictable team both looking dominant in their trio of wins while struggling in most of their six losses. FanDuel Sportsbook currently lists the Chiefs as 3.5-point home favorites.

The Falcons also have the misfortune of not coming off of a European game into their bye week. The Falcons lost 34-17 to the Cincinnati Bengals in Madrid, Spain on Sunday. They also sent starting safety Jessie Bates to the Jacksonville Jaguars ahead of Tuesday's trade deadline.

We also want to know your score prediction! Vote in the poll on The Feed and share your score prediction!

I do worry about the Falcons' pass rush, even if their defense's other two levels were underwhelming even before trading Bates. With the league's two best running backs facing off, I expect a run heavy day that should lead to a fast moving game.

Look for the Chiefs' pass rush to build on their second half against Atlanta quarterback Michael Penix. This will be a game where Atlanta looks game for a couple of drives before the Chiefs overwhelm in route to a victory. The run game will lead to limited possessions for both sides to keep the scoring from getting out of hand.

The Chiefs and Falcons are on opposite ends of the competitive spectrum in the NFL, and this week's game should show that. The Falcons traded away veteran safety Jessie Bates to the contending Jacksonville Jaguars, while the Chiefs traded for wide receiver Josh Downs from the Indianapolis Colts.

Despite a talented core of offensive playmakers, including Bijan Robinson and Drake London, the Falcons are only 3-6, largely because their defense allows over 340 yards per game.

The Falcons are -8 in the turnover-takeaway category, 30th in the NFL, while the Chiefs are tied for 9th at +4. If they can force turnovers, they will be in good shape to win the game.

I don't think Downs will play more than 20 snaps this week, but I would expect the Chiefs to design a few routes for him to get his feet wet in the offense.

I've got the Chiefs winning this one. Josh Downs makes his debut, and I like Mahomes having another guy who can get open underneath, especially now that Atlanta traded away Jessie Bates. The Falcons also traded their starting center, Ryan Neuzil, so I expect Chris Jones to make life real uncomfortable up the middle.

What impressed me most about the Chiefs' 35–12 win over the Jets was how they finished. Kansas City has a habit of letting teams it should beat hang around, and the Jets were within two points at halftime. The Chiefs outscored them 21–0 from there. Kenneth Walker III looked like the player we saw during the 5–0 start, finishing with 143 yards from scrimmage and three touchdowns. To keep Walker rolling, the Chiefs need their receivers to win against man coverage. Seattle and Denver showed what happens when defenses can load the box and trust their corners to hold up one-on-one: the run slows down, and play-action loses its bite. If the receivers make Atlanta pay for that approach, Walker will have more room to run and Patrick Mahomes can get back to attacking off play-action.

Against Atlanta, the key is slowing down Bijan Robinson. That's the operative phrase: slow him down. He's coming off 100 rushing yards and another 47 through the air, so Kansas City has to account for him even when he doesn't get the handoff. Drake London demands attention, and Kyle Pitts can still cause problems for the Chiefs' linebackers and safeties even in a quiet season. But if Kansas City's run defense keeps Robinson from taking over, Sack Nation can build on its five-sack performance by moving Michael Penix Jr. off his spot and forcing him past his first read. I like the Chiefs' chances when Atlanta has to win that way.

Your Kansas City Chiefs head to Atlanta as big favorites against the 2-6 Falcons. Yes, they've lost 4 of their last 5, but this is a dangerous opponent. They've got the league's best RB and a streaky young QB– it's an offense that can get out to an early lead and make things uncomfortable. But, as always, this game will come down to how the Chiefs execute. From the pass protection to the receivers and the short-yardage offense, this team has questions, but they're more than capable. Ideally, they start strong with Walker and Johnson– if the run game gets going, the Chiefs are hard to beat.

The Chiefs enter this game after righting some of their wrongs last week against the Jets, although still with major question marks for a team intent on making a Super Bowl run. The Falcons, meanwhile, are on a two game losing skid after their upset win over the previously unbeaten San Francisco 49ers. I never quite know what to make of the Falcons. They're a team with some of the best offensive weapons in the entire NFL, yet they can't ever seem to put it all together.

Since Jonathan Taylor in week 2, Bijan Robinson will be the best running back the Chiefs have faced all season. The Chiefs have been great defending the run in 2026, and I think they will be able to contain Robinson, but he will still get his yards. Defensively, the Chiefs should also be able to continue the positive momentum they established last week in pressuring the quarterback and leading to mistakes by the Falcons' offense.

I'm excited to see what the Chiefs' offense has in store for new wide receiver Josh Downs. I'm keeping my expectations in check, however, as it always seems like the Chiefs onboard their wide receivers slowly. I expect Downs to play 35-50% of the team's snaps, but they will still be able to generate enough offense to take home the win.

The Atlanta Falcons only show up for primetime games; they are garbage during noon kickoffs. Despite having multiple weapons on offense, namely running back Bijan Robinson and wide receiver Drake London, they can never seem to put it all together in the daytime.

On the other hand, the Chiefs are coming off one of their best performances of the season, when the pass rush finally woke up and bullied a terrible team in the New York Jets. At the end of the day, I would expect the Chiefs offense to continue rolling while they ramp up new acquisition wide receiver Josh Downs.

Atlanta has been a surprisingly competitive team since quarterback Michael Penix Jr. came back. The Falcons have one of the dynamic duos of weapons in running back Bijan Robinson and Drake London. That alongside a decent offensive line has given the Falcons a shot to win any game. Their defense has been surprisingly competent, but after trading Jessie Bates, that will probably fall off.

I expect Atlanta to have some nice drives against the Chiefs, but the game will play out similarly to the Jets game. At some point, the Chiefs pressure will get to a pocket-bound Penix and Atlanta won't be able to catch up offensively. Chiefs pull away in the second half.

If the Falcons hadn't traded away two starters this week, this would feel much more like a spot where the Chiefs could slip up. Still, noon kickoffs have seemed to come with slow starts for Kansas City this year, and this Falcons team still has a reason to fight to stay tied for first place in the NFC South.

However, Atlanta has had more letdowns than triumphs this year, including a couple of weeks ago when the Tampa Bay Buccaneers ran for 342 yards as a team and didn't need a pass to win.

This game should be a comfortable win for Kansas City because of its offense, leaning on the ground game once again to stay in rhtyhm and open up the middle of the field for passes.

All nine panelists predicted the Chiefs to win by more than ten points. Ron (38-20) and Matt (28-13) had the most accurate Week 9 predictions with only 16 total points of error.

Our readers were less optimistic about how Kansas City would perform. Only 50.6% of our readers anticipated a comfortable Chiefs victory, while 37.2% expected Mahomes and Company to narrowly come out on top. 12.2% of respondents expected the Jets to win.

*To calculate a prediction's points of error, the differences between the prediction and the actual score in point spread, home team score, and away team score are added together. For example, a prediction calls for a 17-10 Chiefs win. They end up winning 16-10, so there were two points of error: the point spread was off by one point, the Kansas City score missed by one point, and the opponent's score was predicted correctly. But if the Chiefs lose the game 17-10, there were 28 points of error in the prediction: the point spread was off by 14 (the difference between +7 and -7), and both scores were off by 7.

#2770. Panthers Injury Report & Transactions – Week 10

The Carolina Panthers front office is busy throughout the season with transactions, roster moves, and injury updates. Here's what's happening at Mint Street this week.

For once, the Panthers got some good news on the injury front heading into their Week 10 showdown against the New Orleans Saints.

Offensive lineman Brady Christensen nearly returned from injury last week, but was held out to ensure he was fully ready to go. Hopefully this is the week the versatile player can help plug one of the many, many holes on the Panthers offensive line. He's currently listed as questionable for Sunday.

Speaking of the Panthers offensive line, guard Damien Lewis was a full participant in practice on both Wednesday and Friday, though he was limited on Thursday. The incumbent starter has missed the Panthers last two games. He looks good to go Sunday.

The Panthers have three additional questionable players this week in outside linebacker Jaelan Phillips, cornerback Will Lee, and wide receiver Xavier Legette. Carolina could definitely use all three of them, at least situationally.

Both cornerback Mike Jackson and running back Jonathon Brooks are doubtful, while linebacker Bobby Okereke is out.

Signed LB Claudin Cherelus to the practice squad

The Panthers have brought back linebacker Claudin Cherelus to the practice squad after waiving him back in September with an injury designation. This is Cherelus's fourth season in Carolina after beginning his NFL career with the Panthers as an undrafted free agent back in 2023. He has appeared in 35 games for the Panthers with six starts, registering 61 tackles.

Released WR David Moore then signed to the practice squad

Since joining the Carolina Panthers in 2024, wide receiver David Moore has been a fixture on the roster more for his veteran stability than his actual on-field productivity. This past week the Panthers waived him then re-signed him to the practice squad, just in case the wide receiver room suffers any more injuries. This year Moore has appeared in six games with one reception 27 yards. He has also returned four punts and eight kickoffs this year.

Signed DT Cam Horsley to the practice squad

Cam Horsley joined the Tennessee Titans last year as an undrafted rookie free agent out of Boston College. To his credit, he made the 53-man roster and appeared in one game, playing 14 defensive snaps without recording any stats. Horsley is a diamond in the rough the Panthers will look to polish to provide some additional depth along the defensive interior.

Placed WR Ihmir Smith-Marsette on practice squad-injured

Ihmir Smith-Marsette is a bit of a blast from the past for Panthers fans. He joined the Panthers in 2023, which was his third NFL season at the time. He saw the field, too, appearing in all 17 games that year as Carolina's primary punt returner who also had 11 kick returns. Back in 2023 he also contributed eight receptions for 51 yards and eight carries for 74 more yards. He left Carolina for the New York Jets in 2024, then didn't appear in any games in 2025. The Panthers added him to the practice squad back in September 2026, and now he has an injury designation.

#2769. Jalen Green’s fit alongside Devin Booker remains a major concern in Phoenix

How much data do you need before you're compelled to make a decision?

In the NBA, the answer to that question is as unique as the front offices tasked with making those decisions. There are countless philosophies surrounding what constitutes meaningful data, how much weight to place on sample sizes, and when statistical trends are reliable enough to warrant action. But here we are, 11 games into the new season, and I know I'm not the only person with two functioning eyes beginning to question whether the Jalen Green experiment is going to work in Phoenix.

I'll admit that I initially viewed Green's acquisition as more of a necessity than an intentional pursuit. The Suns knew they needed to move Kevin Durant for a multitude of reasons, eventually found a trade partner that was acceptable to him (which is an entirely separate situation worth exploring), and needed to make the salaries work. In my mind, Green was simply part of the financial equation required to facilitate the deal.

After all, the Houston Rockets had four seasons of data to evaluate Green. Four years of watching his development, assessing his strengths and weaknesses, and determining whether he was someone they wanted to build around moving forward. Their willingness to include him in the trade suggested that perhaps they'd already reached a conclusion about his long-term value.

As Sam Amick revealed in his latest piece for The Athletic, Green wasn't merely a contractual necessity or a convenient piece of salary matching. He was someone the Phoenix Suns actively coveted.

"(It) was never gonna get done without Jalen and Dillon (Brooks) in the deal," Ishbia told me earlier this year. "Those are the two players we wanted. It wasn't, 'Hey, how do we fill a salary with these numbers?' It was, 'In order to have Kevin Durant on your team, I want Dillon Brooks to help me with my culture and my identity. I want Jalen Green as a 23-year-old up-and-coming leading scorer.'"

Last season was supposed to be the year of evaluation for Green, a player who arrived in Phoenix with an unbelievably clean bill of health, only to appear in 32 games due to lower extremity injuries. So there's data point number one: 32 games. Of course, there's only so much you can reasonably take away from that sample, considering Green was essentially joining a race that was already three-quarters of the way finished by the time he got involved.

Which brings us to this season, the one in which the Suns would finally have an opportunity to evaluate what they actually have in Jalen Green. And the early returns? Less than optimal would probably be the polite way of putting it. Getting benched for the entire fourth quarter against the Charlotte Hornets, a team Green should theoretically thrive against, might be a more accurate description of where things currently stand.

And quite honestly, it was the right decision.

The Suns had been cooking, putting together an impressive 38-16 run in the first half and establishing the kind of offensive rhythm that has been painfully difficult to sustain this season. Much of this occurred with Green on the bench. But when Green checked into the game with 6:06 remaining in the second quarter, that momentum came to a screeching halt.

Charlotte proceeded to unleash a 31-11 run that extended into the third quarter, completely erasing the progress Phoenix had made. During Green's stint on the floor, from 6:06 remaining in the second quarter until he was substituted out with 5:26 left in the third, the Suns were outscored by 18 points. And that was the last we saw of Jalen Green.

Once again, Phoenix struggled to generate productive minutes with Green on the floor, and I believe that gets to the heart of the problem. It's becoming increasingly difficult to identify how his presence complements the players around him, particularly when the team appears to function more effectively without him. And for a player the organization actively coveted as a centerpiece of the Kevin Durant trade, that's a pretty troubling development.

No one is questioning Jalen Green's athleticism. No one is questioning his desire, his attitude, his likability, or his character. By all accounts, he's a stand-up guy who has reached the highest level of professional basketball largely because of his extraordinary physical gifts. His explosiveness, speed, and ability to create separation are undeniable.

But when you reach the NBA, physical gifts can only take you so far. At some point, the difference between being a good player and becoming a great one comes down to your ability to understand the game, recognize situations, make the right decisions, and contribute within the structure of a functioning offense.

When you're the second highest paid player on the roster, earning $36.3 million this season, the Phoenix Suns need you to be great. They need you on the floor in the fourth quarter, positively influencing the outcome of basketball games. They need you making intelligent decisions, creating opportunities for your teammates, and understanding when to attack and when to allow the offense to develop.

What they don't need is someone disrupting the offensive rhythm, launching ill-timed three-point attempts that have little connection to what the team is trying to accomplish, or throwing the basketball directly into the hands of the opposition. Unfortunately, we've seen far too much of the latter. Green, who ranks 53rd in salary this season, is ranked 113th in scoring (11.8 points per game), 92nd in assists (3.1 assists), 189th in three-point percentage (27.0%), and 246th in NET +/- (-28.0).

The Ringer's Rob Mahoney recently said on the Bill Simmons podcast that Green has gone "from counterproductive to actively bad this season." I can't say I disagree.

Perhaps some of these struggles can be attributed to the Suns asking Green to operate in a role he's never truly occupied before, that of a primary facilitator. It's a reasonable explanation, considering his development in Houston was largely centered around his ability to score rather than orchestrate an offense. But as the games have progressed, I'm beginning to wonder whether that's even what Phoenix is asking him to do anymore.

His assist percentage currently sits at 17.1%, placing him in the 44th percentile this season. That doesn't exactly suggest a player being tasked with consistently running the offense. It feels more like the Suns want him to occasionally facilitate, score when opportunities arise, and somehow make everything fit together. Therein lies the problem. Green's presence continues to look awkward within this offense's structure. His movements feel disconnected from those around him, his decisions frequently interrupt the flow of possessions, and his inability to consistently operate within the team's offensive framework has become increasingly detrimental.

That's what makes this entire situation such a quagmire. Green is struggling to operate within the confines of everything happening around him, and much of what we're witnessing mirrors the problems we saw last season. At the time, we could point to his injuries, the late start, and the lack of continuity as reasonable explanations for his struggles. Those were legitimate factors, and they made it difficult to draw any definitive conclusions.

But now? We're beginning to see some familiar patterns emerge. And the Suns, as they seem to be in almost every avenue concerning their ability to build for the future, find themselves stuck between a rock and a hard place.

Green's trade value isn't particularly encouraging at the moment. If Phoenix were to explore moving him, the return would likely be underwhelming. Any opposing team willing to absorb his $36.3 million salary would have to believe there's something worth salvaging, and even with his contract becoming an expiring deal next season, there's a reasonable chance that team would want draft compensation to make the transaction worthwhile.

What exactly are the Suns supposed to offer? They don't have an abundance of desirable draft assets sitting around, waiting to be attached to another expensive contract in hopes of making a problem disappear. They used those up to get off of the Nurkic deal or to acquire Mark Williams and Miles Bridges. We've already watched this organization sacrifice enough of its future trying to correct mistakes from its past.

So what do you do? One possibility is moving Green to the bench, and quite honestly, that might be the most sensible solution available. We've seen a version of this experiment before. In January 2025, Mike Budenholzer finally came to the realization that pairing Devin Booker with another ball dominant shooting guard in Bradley Beal was creating more problems than it was solving. The overlapping skill sets were limiting the team's offensive ceiling, and something needed to change.

We all remember how that went. Beal wasn't particularly receptive to the adjustment, and the situation became another chapter in the ongoing dysfunction surrounding that roster.

Green, to his credit, appears to have a different attitude toward making adjustments. He's expressed a willingness to evolve his game, embrace more facilitating responsibilities, and has spent time working with Steve Nash to improve that aspect of his skill set. Everything suggests he wants to fit in, wants to improve, and wants to become the player Phoenix envisioned when it acquired him. I don't question any of that. But wanting something to work doesn't necessarily mean it's going to work.

When you're playing alongside Devin Booker, the fit becomes especially problematic. Booker possesses an extraordinary ability to control an offense, manipulate defenses, and create scoring opportunities for himself and others. Those are his superpowers. The players surrounding him should be enhancing those abilities, creating space for him to operate, and making his job easier. They shouldn't be obstacles he has to navigate.

Perhaps the answer is allowing Green to find his footing with the second unit, where he can operate with greater freedom, develop some confidence, and establish a rhythm without constantly having to figure out how his game intersects with Booker's. If he begins finding success in that role, maybe the comfort and confidence he develops can eventually carry over into shared minutes with the starters.

Of course, that raises another question. Who starts in his place? Collin Gillespie would seem to be the most obvious candidate, given his familiarity with the offense, his ability to play alongside Booker, and the success Phoenix has experienced with him on the floor. But at this point, you could certainly sell me on Jordan Goodwin as well. Goodwin might not be the traditional facilitator you're looking for, but he brings something this team desperately needs: a player who can positively influence the game on both ends of the floor without requiring the offense to revolve around him. That's a conversation worth exploring another day.

Ultimately, as I sat there watching the fourth quarter against Charlotte, knowing how ineffective Green had been throughout his minutes, I found myself relieved that he wasn't on the floor. And I don't particularly enjoy admitting that. Here was a player the Suns actively pursued as part of the Kevin Durant trade, someone they envisioned becoming a significant part of their future, and I was genuinely pleased that he was sitting on the bench during the most important stretch of the game.

Instead, Phoenix leaned on a group that looked much more familiar. Much of the lineup resembled the units that helped the Suns win so many games last season, with Luke Kennard occupying the role previously filled by Grayson Allen for much of the stretch. And what happened? The Suns played well. The offense flowed. Players understood their responsibilities, the ball moved with purpose, and Phoenix outscored Charlotte 29-20 in a fourth quarter that was void of his presence.

There was a familiarity to it, a sense that the players on the floor understood how to complement one another rather than compete for the same opportunities. And that's what makes this entire situation so frustrating. The Suns have invested significant money, opportunity, and organizational belief in Jalen Green. They want him to succeed. They need him to succeed. But right now, some of their most effective basketball is being played when he's watching from the sideline.

Eleven games into the season might not be enough data to make a definitive judgment about his future. But it's certainly enough to start asking some uncomfortable questions.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2768. JJ Redick Provides Key Updates On Injured Lakers Days Before Suns Game

The Los Angeles Lakers (11-2) currently lead the Western Conference standings, but they face multiple injuries ahead of Sunday's game against the Phoenix Suns (5-6). Fortunately, they've avoided any long-term setbacks, but Lakers fans are still concerned about the team's current state.

That's why, on Friday, head coach JJ Redick provided clarity in a chat with the media, revealing the latest development with each of the four injured Lakers guards: Austin Reaves, Luka Doncic, Collin Sexton, and Quentin Grimes.

"Luka is fine," said Redick on Friday, via Mark Medina. "The expectation would be that he's available. AR is based on his physical evaluation and how he was feeling yesterday. We opted against imaging; he'll be day-to-day. We'll have him on the report tomorrow at 5:00. Collin is in the same boat. Concussion's been ruled out; he ended up getting stitches, and he'll be day-to-day. And then Q [Quentin Grimes] was able to do the live part of the stay-ready game."

Plenty of NBA teams have been plagued by injuries this season, and the Lakers are no exception. Since opening night, they've had various players in and out of the lineup, but it hasn't made much of a difference. Thanks to Redick's coaching, a loaded roster, and heroics from Luka (averaging 38.6 points, 7.5 rebounds, 7.6 assists, 2.0 steals, and 0.7 blocks per game on 48.9% shooting), they've been a dominant force in the West, but things could always take a turn for the worse.

In that one game against the Milwaukee Bucks on November 11th, four different players got banged up, including Doncic himself. The MVP frontrunner turned his ankle late in the fourth quarter on Wednesday, raising fears he could miss time. Fortunately, he woke up pain-free the following morning, all but sealing his active status for Sunday's game.

It's a similar story with guard Quentin Grimes, who has provided a major boost off the bench with averages of 8.0 points, 2.5 rebounds, 2.5 assists, 0.9 steals, and 0.2 blocks per game on 43.1% shooting from the field. With a sprained finger, his condition was never serious, and Redick confirmed today that he was well enough to participate in live contact drills.

6'5″ guard Austin Reaves is facing a slightly more serious situation. He only played 25 minutes against the Bucks after landing awkwardly on a dunk in the third quarter. He tested negative for an Achilles injury and opted out of further tests as he works toward a quick return. He's currently day-to-day, suggesting that he might need more time to get his body right. This season, in 12 games, he's averaging 24.7 points, 3.3 rebounds, 4.7 assists, 1.2 steals, and 0.3 blocks per game on 51.3% shooting from the field.

For Collin Sexton, concussion protocol was activated after a head-to-head collision with Ryan Rollins. He needed stitches to fix a cut above his right eye, but the Lakers confirmed that he suffered no other complications. He should be cleared to play on Sunday, but there's still a chance he could sit if the Lakers prefer to be extra cautious.

With the rest of today and all of tomorrow to rest and heal, the Lakers are likely at full strength for their next game, and we know what that means for their chances of victory. What's better, with showdowns against the Hornets, Jazz, and Kings on their schedule, this next stretch of games could easily extend their win streak.

As a corresponding move, the Texans have released running back Dare Ogunbowale, who is subject to waivers now that the trading deadline has passed.

#2764. A.J. Brown, Rhamondre Stevenson among Patriots questionable for Sunday vs. Lions

The Patriots have several players listed as questionable for Sunday’s game against the Lions in Madrid.

A.J. Brown (ankle/knee) and running back Rhamondre Stevenson (knee) are among them.

Brown was listed as limited on all three injury reports this week. Stevenson was limited on Thursday and Friday after he was a non-participant on Wednesday.

Safety Kevin Byard (chest), fullback Reggie Gilliam (knee), tight end Cameron Latu (back), offensive lineman Walter Rouse (concussion protocol), offensive lineman Greg Van Roten (hamstring), and cornerback Charles Woods (hamstring) are also questionable.

Offensive tackle Morgan Moses (foot), tight end Hunter Henry (foot), and quarterback Drake Maye (right shoulder) are all off the injury report and are set to play.

Offensive lineman Ben Brown (knee), cornerback Carlton Davis (knee), and running back TreVeyon Henderson (illness) were ruled out earlier this week as they didn’t travel to Europe.

#2762. Kirk Cousins will start for Vikings on Sunday

Vikings head coach Kevin O'Connell wouldn't commit to Kirk Cousins as the team's starter earlier this week. O'Connell made it official on Friday, though, announcing Cousins will start against the Packers.

The Vikings acquired Cousins from the Raiders at Tuesday's trade deadline, in exchange for a 2027 third-round pick. He practiced with the team Thursday and Friday, but Cousins' familiarity with the offense allowed him to step right into the starting job.

Cousins was with the Vikings from 2018-23, the last two years with O'Connell. The two were also together in 2017 with Washington.

Kyler Murray started six games before he injured his hamstring. Carson Wentz started Monday night's loss to the Bills and had the worst game by a Vikings' starting quarterback since 2006.

Murray is ruled out for Sunday's game after not practicing all week.

The Vikings also ruled out linebacker Blake Cashman (concussion), tight end T.J. Hockenson (wrist/chest), defensive lineman Tyrion Ingram-Dawkins (ankle) and wide receiver Jauan Jennings (calf).

Outside linebacker Kyle Van Noy (calf), wide receiver Jordan Addison (thumb) and safety Josh Metellus (shoulder) are questionable.
